with aliasing. The control point is what stops a draw that floods the target from passing.

### Mutation evidence, run twice by two parties

Reverting the production fix (`chunks[particle_calls_[frame_]++]` → `chunks[0]`):

```
REQUIRE( max_rgb_delta > min_channel_delta )
with expansion:  0 > 64
  samples: P_A(32,32)=(0, 0, 0, 255) P_B(96,96)=(115, 223, 232, 255) P_control(32,96)=(0, 0, 0, 255)
```

P_A reads exactly background while P_B stays lit — the predicted signature of both draws
reading the second batch. The implementer ran it; the reviewer then reverted the
production line independently and reproduced identical numbers on a different random seed
(2970179147 vs 2062692333). **Status is not evidence; the failing assertion and its
numbers are.** Restored with `touch` afterwards, because `mv`/`git checkout` preserve mtime
and ninja will otherwise keep the mutant binary and produce bogus failures later.

### Still open, with dependencies named

`draw_ribbons` and `draw_world_text` (same shape; the latter needs a non-empty atlas via
`create_texture`, as it early-returns on `textures_.empty()`), and the `begin_frame`
counter resets — which are **not** pixel-observable, because un-reset counters allocate
*distinct* buffers rather than aliasing, making the failure unbounded memory growth.

## The HUD underlay, asserted in pixels instead of by eye

**Shipped 2026-10-24 (PR #304).** The demo's underlay-beneath-checker claim had been
confirmed exactly once, by a human at a window on 2026-10-12.

### It was not untested — it was tested one layer too high

`tests/unit/app/demo_scene_test.cpp:116` already asserted the underlay is *submitted*
first, through a recording device, identified by tint. That is submission order. It cannot
see whether the GPU composites in that order: a blend-state or pipeline change could
invert what reaches the screen while the test stayed green. **The two tests now chain**: the old one
proves the demo submits underlay-first (`order = -1` vs `0`), the new one proves that
submitting first means appearing beneath. Neither alone closes the loop. The old test was
deliberately left untouched.

### A probe that was rejected for being a no-op

Reversing the per-quad loop in the backend's `draw_ui_image` (`vulkan_render_device.cpp:1886`)
looks like a production-side mutation and would have been the stronger probe. It is not
one: every call in the tests *and* in the real image pass carries exactly one quad, because
that pass draws per entity with its own texture handle and cannot batch. The edit emits no
behavioural change and the test would have "passed" against it — proving nothing while
looking rigorous. The discriminator used instead was swapping the two `draw_ui_image` calls,
which failed on the overlap assertion by 88 in the red channel, reproduced independently.

The gap that probe exposed — ordering *within* one call's quad vector — is now its own
backlog entry, with the trigger being the first call site that batches more than one quad.

## Mesa lavapipe probed as a software ICD for the GPU tests

**Probed 2026-10-24; the CI change itself was deliberately NOT made.** The opt-in GPU
tests guard nothing automatically, and slice 1 recorded the trigger for making them
standing as "a CI runner with a GPU" — too narrow, since a software rasteriser would do.

### All three unknowns cleared, and the cost prediction was inverted

| Question | Predicted | Achieved |
|---|---|---|
| Conformance | 4/4 pass, ±1 ULP on sRGB | **4/4, then 6/6 with the day's new tests.** 0 ULP on clears; the ±1 tolerance absorbed a 1-ULP difference that **both** drivers show against CPU `std::pow`, in opposite directions |
| Cost | 2-5x **slower** (LLVM JIT) | **2.5x-9.2x faster** |
| False failures | 0 | 0, including under `MOSAIC_VK_SYNC_VALIDATION=1` |

**The gap worth keeping is the cost one.** The prediction assumed shader compilation and
fillrate dominate. They do not: these tests are dominated by six-to-seven device
bring-up/teardown cycles, where NVIDIA pays kernel ioctl and context setup (~150-300ms)
and a user-space rasteriser pays ~20-45ms. The lesson generalises past lavapipe — *what a
fast run is made of* is device creation, so anything optimising pixel throughput here is
aimed at the wrong thing.

### The evidence was briefly unreproducible, which is itself the finding

The probe was run against a lavapipe extracted into a scratch directory that did not
survive the session, and the findings document as first written recorded the measurements
but not the provisioning steps. Nothing could be re-run; the evidence existed only as
pasted output. **Re-deriving the setup from scratch during review is what confirmed the
result** — 7/7 device creations on llvmpipe, 5/5 tests in 0.25s against 1.05s on NVIDIA
(4.2x, inside the claimed range). The reproduction steps are now in the findings document.

### Two CI traps found by reading the workflow, not by running it

Enabling the GPU tests in CI fails the build in two places that have nothing to do with
Vulkan: `ci.yml:177` passes only `mosaic_unit_tests-*_tests.cmake` to `check_ctest_run.py`
so O1 fails on the count mismatch, and `check_next_steps_claims.py` fails unless
`next-steps.md`'s suite-count rows move in lockstep. Both are recorded in the backlog entry
for the CI change, which was kept as a separate slice rather than bolted onto the two test
PRs that shipped the same day.

## The GPU tests join CI on a software ICD, as a trial (2026-10-24)

The [lavapipe probe](../project/lavapipe-probe-findings.md) recommended a trial PR and named
two traps that would break the build. The trial is this change. Neither trap was worked
around; both were made unreachable.

**The design decision: a separate step, not a bigger suite.** The obvious move was to let the
existing `ctest` invocation discover all 1468 tests and widen
`check_ctest_run.py`'s glob to `mosaic_*_tests-*_tests.cmake`. That works, and it costs both
standing guards their meaning:

- `check_ctest_run.py`'s O1 compares `ctest -N` against the `add_test` lines in the files it
  is handed. One suite, one glob, one denominator — widen it and a GPU test vanishing is
  invisible as long as the total still matches.
- `check_next_steps_claims.py` verifies the handoff's `Suite size` / `Suite status` rows
  against the JUnit file. Those rows would have had to read 1468 — a number **no default
  local build produces**, since `MOSAIC_ENABLE_GPU_TESTS` is `OFF`. The handoff's own
  contract is "every claim carries the command that regenerates it", and the regenerating
  command would have disagreed with the text for every developer who ran it.

So the suites are split by a ctest **label**, not a name match: `catch_discover_tests(... LABELS
gpu)` in `tests/CMakeLists.txt`. CI's unit step runs `-LE gpu`, the new step runs `-L gpu`.
Measured locally: `1462` and `6`, against `1462` and `6` `add_test` lines in the two generated
files. Each suite keeps an exact denominator, the handoff keeps saying 1462 truthfully, and
neither guard was touched.

**What was proven before the step was trusted.** The probe's own discipline — prove the device
identity before believing any number — is now a CI step rather than a paragraph. It asserts
every `Using "..."` line in the loader trace is `llvmpipe` and fails otherwise. It carries both
controls, which is the lesson from the dead-anchor retraction one entry up: it passes on the
lavapipe ICD (2 of 2 llvmpipe) and was **confirmed to fail** when run against the NVIDIA driver
(2 of 2 NVIDIA). A guard that only ever saw the good case would not have been evidence.

**The vacuous-green question, answered by measurement.** A GPU test that cannot reach a device
could in principle skip, which `check_ctest_run.py`'s O2 would catch — but it is better if the
case never arises. Measured both ways locally: `VK_ICD_FILENAMES` pointing at a nonexistent
manifest, and at a manifest whose `library_path` points nowhere. Both fail **6 of 6** on
`vkCreateInstance failed: VkResult -9`. A missing ICD on the runner is loud. O2's skip guard
was not relaxed, as the backlog required.

**What this trial measures, and what still cannot be known from here.** The probe's numbers came
from Arch's Mesa 26.2.2 in a scratch directory. The runner ships an older
`mesa-vulkan-drivers`, and names the manifest `lvp_icd.x86_64.json` rather than `lvp_icd.json`.
Two figures were unmeasurable locally and are exactly what the trial reports: the runner's Mesa
version (printed by the install step) and the `apt-get` wall-clock cost, which the probe
estimated at ~3-10s against a ~1m20s job. That estimate, if right, **exceeds the test execution
it buys** — locally the 6 GPU tests ran in 0.11s. The case for the trial was never speed; it is
that a GPU-less runner can assert pixels at all.

**The first run was red, and that is the trial working.** Run 1 failed at the device-identity
step on `test -f /usr/share/vulkan/icd.d/lvp_icd.x86_64.json`. The probe document, and the
backlog entry written from it, both stated that Ubuntu names the manifest with an `.x86_64`
infix where Arch does not. **That claim was never observed on a runner, and it is false:**
`mesa-vulkan-drivers 25.2.8-0ubuntu0.24.04.4` on noble ships `lvp_icd.json`, with no infix,
exactly as Arch does. The step failed loudly and printed the directory listing, which is how
it was caught in one run rather than by guesswork; the workflow now globs `lvp_icd*.json`, and
the claim is retracted at both sources. Worth noting the shape of this: the assertion was
confidently written, internally consistent, and repeated into a second document before anything
checked it — the same failure mode as the dead-anchor claim retracted two entries up.

**The two unmeasurable figures, measured** (run
[40144987504](https://github.com/Evol-Luci/mosaic/actions/runs/40144987504), green):

| Step | Cost |
|---|---:|
| `apt-get` for `mesa-vulkan-drivers` + `vulkan-validationlayers` (23.4 MB) | **3s** |
| Lavapipe device identity | **1s** |
| GPU tests under lavapipe (6 cases, `0.55s` ctest real) | **<1s** |
| **Total added** | **~4s** |

The runner's Mesa is **25.2.8**, not the ~24.0/24.2 the probe guessed, with **LLVM 20.1.2**
against 22.1.8 locally. Validation layers come from LunarG (`1.4.313.0~rc2`), not Ubuntu. The
job ran 1m10s; `main` runs over the preceding week ranged 83-121s, so **~4s is inside
run-to-run variance and cannot be resolved against it** — do not read the 1m10s as a speedup.

How far inside: a later commit on this same branch, changing only Markdown, took **3m22s** with
every step inflated 3-4x — the LunarG install 11s to 66s, the build 16s to 49s, and the identity
step 1s to 15s doing byte-identical work. Nothing in the trial caused it; the runner was simply
slow. Which is the honest frame for the whole cost question: **runner variance is an order of
magnitude larger than the 4s this change adds**, so if the apt cost is ever judged too high it
will have to be judged on the principle, not on a job-total measurement, because a job total
cannot resolve it.

The probe's two real conformance unknowns both resolved in its favour on the older stack: all
6 cases passed, including the `±1` sRGB tolerance cases, and the tests' own hard assertions on
`validation_enabled()` and an empty validation-message list held. No driver-specific validation
message appeared.

**The green was read, not trusted.** The step logs confirm `lavapipe ICD manifest:
/usr/share/vulkan/icd.d/lvp_icd.json`, `2 Using "llvmpipe (LLVM 20.1.2, 256 bits)"`,
`check_ctest_run: OK, 6 of 6`, and separately `check_ctest_run: OK, 1462 of 1462` — the split
held on the runner exactly as measured locally.

**The revert condition, unchanged from the probe.** If the runner's older Mesa emits validation
messages that hardware drivers do not, or the apt cost is judged too high, delete the two trial
steps, the install step and the `-DMOSAIC_ENABLE_GPU_TESTS=ON` flag. Nothing else depends on
them; the `LABELS gpu` tag and the `-LE gpu` filter are harmless on their own.

## `draw_ribbons` and `draw_world_text` per-call buffer aliasing asserted in pixels (2026-10-25)

**Shipped 2026-10-25.** Closes the remaining two paths of the per-frame buffer-aliasing class
(#275, #287) with `tests/gpu/offscreen_ribbon_aliasing_test.cpp` and
`tests/gpu/offscreen_world_text_aliasing_test.cpp`, modelled on
`offscreen_particle_aliasing_test.cpp` (#303). Spec:
`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-25-gpu-ribbon-world-text-aliasing-design.md`.

### Why existing unit tests did not cover either path

- `tests/unit/render/render_system_test.cpp` tests `RenderSystem`'s call grouping against
  `FakeRenderDevice` with no `VkDevice`. Its `FakeRenderDevice::draw_world_text` (`:222-224`)
  deliberately models the *unfixed* single-buffer overwrite at offset 0 to force same-atlas
  batching in `RenderSystem`, and the multi-font test (`:1504-1539`) inspects the recorded call
  vector `device.world_text_draws` rather than executed GPU draws.
- `tests/unit/render/texture_colorspace_contract_test.cpp` (`:345-392`) checks the normalised
  source text of `vulkan_render_device.cpp` for `chunks.emplace_back();` and `_calls_[frame_]++]`
  without creating a `VkDevice` or executing a draw.
- `draw_ribbons` aliasing is **latent** at the `RenderSystem` level today (`RenderSystem`
  accumulates one `RibbonVertex` vector across all `TrailRenderer` entities and calls
  `draw_ribbons` once per frame), whereas `draw_world_text` is called once per distinct font
  atlas per frame. Both are tested directly at the `RenderDevice` level with two calls in one
  frame inside the HDR scene pass (`begin_hdr_scene_pass` → draw A → draw B →
  `end_hdr_scene_pass` → `draw_hdr_resolve(1.0f, 0.0f)`). For `draw_world_text`, a 4x4 RGBA8
  solid-white texture is uploaded via `create_texture` first so the `textures_.empty()` guard
  passes and atlas alpha is 1.0 everywhere.

### Predicted vs. achieved (including revert probes)

| Check | Predicted | Achieved |
|---|---|---|
| Unmodified `draw_ribbons` (`P_A`, `P_B`, `P_control`) | `P_A` and `P_B` differ from `(0,0,0,255)` by `> 64`; `P_control` within `±1` of `(0,0,0,255)`; 0 validation messages | `P_A(32,32)=(232, 165, 115, 255)`, `P_B(96,96)=(115, 223, 232, 255)`, `P_control(32,96)=(0, 0, 0, 255)`, 0 validation messages |
| Unmodified `draw_world_text` (`P_A`, `P_B`, `P_control`) | Same positional oracle | `P_A(32,32)=(232, 165, 115, 255)`, `P_B(96,96)=(115, 223, 232, 255)`, `P_control(32,96)=(0, 0, 0, 255)`, 0 validation messages |
| Revert probe: `draw_ribbons` (`chunks[ribbon_calls_[frame_]++]` → `chunks[0]`) | RED on `P_A (first draw_ribbons call)` with `P_A=(0, 0, 0, 255)` (`0 > 64`), `P_B` lit, `P_control` black | RED at `offscreen_ribbon_aliasing_test.cpp:127`: `REQUIRE( max_rgb_delta > min_channel_delta )` (`0 > 64`), `P_A(32,32)=(0, 0, 0, 255)`, `P_B(96,96)=(115, 223, 232, 255)`, `P_control(32,96)=(0, 0, 0, 255)` |
| Revert probe: `draw_world_text` (`chunks[world_text_calls_[frame_]++]` → `chunks[0]`) | RED on `P_A (first draw_world_text call)` with `P_A=(0, 0, 0, 255)` (`0 > 64`), `P_B` lit, `P_control` black | RED at `offscreen_world_text_aliasing_test.cpp:128`: `REQUIRE( max_rgb_delta > min_channel_delta )` (`0 > 64`), `P_A(32,32)=(0, 0, 0, 255)`, `P_B(96,96)=(115, 223, 232, 255)`, `P_control(32,96)=(0, 0, 0, 255)` |
| Default build (`MOSAIC_ENABLE_GPU_TESTS=OFF`) | 1462 tests discovered and passing | 1462/1462 |
| GPU build (`-DMOSAIC_ENABLE_GPU_TESTS=ON`, `-L gpu`) | 8 GPU tests discovered and passing | 8/8 |

### GPU pixel-assertion inventory (2026-10-25)

Moved out of `next-steps.md` on 2026-10-25, which was carrying this list inline and had
reached 7.7 KB against its own 8 KB ceiling. The handoff keeps the operational facts (how
to build and run the GPU tests, that opt-in locally must not be relaxed, why the suite rows
still say 1462) and points here for the inventory, because the inventory grows with every
slice and the handoff must not.

**Asserted in pixels, against the real `VulkanRenderDevice` rendering offscreen:**

| Class | Test | Shipped |
|---|---|---|
| `draw_ui` quad placement, top-left origin (`ui.vert`'s Y-flip compensation) | `tests/gpu/offscreen_ui_orientation_test.cpp` | 2026-10-24, mutation-proven |
| Per-call buffer chunking, `draw_particles` | `tests/gpu/offscreen_particle_aliasing_test.cpp` | PR #303 |
| Per-call buffer chunking, `draw_ribbons` | `tests/gpu/offscreen_ribbon_aliasing_test.cpp` | 2026-10-25 |
| Per-call buffer chunking, `draw_world_text` | `tests/gpu/offscreen_world_text_aliasing_test.cpp` | 2026-10-25 |
| HUD underlay composite order (`draw_ui_image` beneath the checker) | `tests/gpu/offscreen_hud_underlay_test.cpp` | PR #304 |

Plus three device-level tests (`headless_device_test.cpp`, `offscreen_readback_test.cpp`,
`offscreen_render_device_test.cpp`): 8 under `-L gpu` in total.

**Still NOT asserted, each for a stated reason:**

- **`screen_effect.vert`'s missing Y-flip compensation.** Genuinely wrong, but not
  pixel-observable — the effects it drives are a uniform tint and a symmetric vignette, so a
  flipped sample lands on an identical value. Deliberately untested; the backlog carries the
  trigger that would make it observable (an asymmetric or off-centre effect).
- **The `begin_frame` per-call counter resets** (`particle_calls_`, `ribbon_calls_`,
  `world_text_calls_` at `vulkan_render_device.cpp:1931-1933`). Omitting a reset allocates
  *distinct* buffers per call across frames rather than aliasing within one, so the failure
  mode is unbounded memory growth, not a wrong pixel. Needs a chunk-count inspection seam;
  only a source-text contract test covers it today.
- **Ordering within a single `draw_ui_image` call's quad vector.** Found during PR #304
  review: reversing the per-quad loop is a no-op edit against the current tests.

**The rule, restated:** GPU tests are opt-in *locally* (`-DMOSAIC_ENABLE_GPU_TESTS=ON`) and
that must not be relaxed, but they are **not** skipped — CI runs them on Mesa lavapipe in
their own step, labelled `gpu`, as a trial (#307). The unit step filters `-LE gpu` and the
lavapipe step `-L gpu`, so each keeps its own denominator and neither can silently absorb
the other. Both regression gates are defined in full in
[two gates, defined precisely](#two-gates-defined-precisely-moved-out-of-the-handoff-2026-10-24).

**The one claim that could not be proven locally, now proven.** The two aliasing tests were
written and mutation-probed on real hardware (an NVIDIA RTX 3080), so whether their oracles
survived a software rasteriser was genuinely open: the thresholds are tolerance-based
(`max_rgb_delta > 64` for a lit sample, within ±1 of black for the control) rather than exact
tone-mapped values, which is *why* they were expected to hold, but expectation is not
observation. PR #309's own CI run settled it — `gpu offscreen draw_ribbons ...` (#1468) and
`gpu offscreen draw_world_text ...` (#1469) both passed under Mesa lavapipe, 8/8 in the `gpu`
step, at 0.58 s and 0.59 s against 0.14 s locally. Two notes for whoever reads this next: the
prediction that it would take a `main` run to prove was wrong, because the lavapipe step runs
on pull requests too; and a green `gpu` step is not by itself evidence, since a step that
silently discovered 6 tests instead of 8 would look identical — the count and the test names
in the log are the evidence.

## The 2026-10-25 tier re-derivation: a pass whose finding was how little it found

**SHIPPED 2026-10-25 (#309).** Due by the standing rule — 24 PRs (#286-#309) had merged since
the previous pass's own PR (#285), including the entire offscreen-render arc, the PCH, and the
lavapipe CI trial. The mechanical result is three new rows (one 🟢, two 🟡), one 🟡 row struck as
complete, and one 🔴 row re-rated.

**The finding is the ratio.** Of roughly 120 live top-level bullets, **15 carried new evidence
from those 24 PRs, and only 3 of the 15 were rankable work.** The other 12 were notes, probes
and declines the render arc produced on its way past: `VK_EXT_headless_surface` is unavailable
on this machine (probed, so nobody re-probes it), unity builds declined with a trigger,
`GIT_SHALLOW` on `box3d` declined with the CMake reason, `screen_effect.vert` latent and
deliberately untested.

That is not a shortfall, and it is worth stating plainly because the obvious reading of a thin
pass is that the pass was done badly. **A slice that closes its own named successors as it goes
leaves declines behind rather than a queue.** The previous pass had 25 live bullets of new
material and found 19 entries misreporting their own state; this one found none, because
#285-#308 fixed that class and the arc since then closed its successors on the way. The honest
consequence: the 🟢/🟡 tables are now **all maintenance of things that already exist**, and the
🔴 table is the real answer to "what is next".

**The one re-rating.** *Test selection / caching test results* has been 🔴/low-priority for
weeks on the premise of a slow suite. Parallel `ctest` (#273, #288) and the IBL-bake fix took
the local suite to 3.8 s and the CI step to ~11-18 s, so the entire addressable saving is now
seconds against change-impact analysis as the cost. Re-rated to **Low, and close to pointless**,
with a reopening trigger rather than a deletion. This is the first time a tier row was re-rated
because *other* work removed its justification rather than because new work raised its value —
worth watching for, since a row whose premise has quietly expired reads exactly like a row
nobody has gotten to yet.

**The new 🟡 rows are both about guarding claims rather than adding features.** Row 5 is the
`begin_frame` counter-reset seam: the last non-latent way to reintroduce the #275/#287 aliasing
class with every test still green, and it needs a chunk-count inspection seam because the
failure mode is unbounded memory growth, not a wrong pixel. Rows 6 and 7 are the two halves of
`next-steps.md`'s State table that #290 left on the honour system — the `Checked` freshness
column, and the one row still declared `UNVERIFIABLE`.

**Predicted vs achieved.** Predicted ~6-8 rankable candidates from 24 PRs, mostly from the
render arc; achieved 3, none of them from the render arc's own subject matter. The wrong
assumption was that a large arc generates proportional follow-on work — it generates follow-on
*declines* when each slice names and closes its successors, which is the behaviour the handoff
and backlog conventions were built to encourage. Backlog size 164 KB → 167 KB (predicted growth,
since a pass adds rows and strikes rather than removing text).

## The 2026-10-26 tier re-derivation: the UI pillar's next group is the question

**SHIPPED 2026-10-26.** Due by the standing rule — 8 PRs (#310-#317) had merged since the
previous pass rode in on #309: the whole U8 remainder arc (#310 the decomposition, #311 U8b,
#312 U8c, #313 U8d, #317 U8e), the `field_bool` slice (#316) and two docs slices (#314, #315).
The mechanical result is **two new rows (one 🟢, one 🟡), two new 🔴 rows, one 🔴 row finalised,
one "decided: no action" row added, and two defects fixed in the tables themselves.**

**The ratio repeated, which makes it a shape rather than an artefact.** Those 8 PRs created
**13 live top-level bullets and struck 7**, against 133 live overall. Of the 13, **two were
rankable agent work**; nine are trigger-gated declines the U8 arc left behind as it closed its
own successors, and two need a human at a display. The previous pass found 3 rankable of 15 and
called that ratio its finding while allowing it might be an artefact of one large arc. A second
consecutive window at the same ratio, from a completely different kind of arc, says it is the
normal growth shape of this backlog: *slices that name and close their successors leave declines,
not a queue.*

**The actual subject of this pass was the hole U8's completion opened.** The previous pass ended
with "the 🔴 table is the real answer to what is next, and U8 is the near-term row in it". U8 is
now complete across five slices, so that answer is spent — and because the U8 row had stood for
the entire UI pillar, the 🔴 table was left with **no UI row at all** while three of the pillar's
eleven groups remain unbuilt. U9 and U11 are now rows of their own. This is explicitly **not** a
re-rating (nothing else in the window gained evidence); it is a gap this pass's own completion
created, and it is worth recording because a pillar represented by one row disappears silently
when that row ships.

**The two candidates pull in opposite directions, and the pass deliberately does not pick.**
*U9 (clipping and scroll)* is the **enabling** one: `grep -ri scissor engine/render/` returns
nothing, so it is a renderer change before it is a UI change, and three live UI items are queued
behind it — a grid's overflow children can only vanish today because clip and scroll both need
the hook, and the `PointerState` "inside the window" widening is shared with U9's own need for
wheel and multi-button. *U11 (text quality)* is the **unblocked** one and carries a live defect:
`layout_text` skips any codepoint the atlas lacks (`text_layout.hpp:54`), the atlas is cooked for
codepoints 32-126 only (`font_cook.cpp:25`), and both the measure and place passes skip alike —
so "Café" renders as "Caf" with the centring quietly recomputed and no warning anywhere. Enabling
capability against latent correctness is a judgement about what the project is for, not a ranking
the tables can derive, so both rows state their own counter-argument and the choice went back to
the human. **U10 was deliberately left out**: it is blocked on an editor input provider, and
starting it would ship navigation nobody can drive.

**Two defects were in the tables themselves, both introduced by me.** The 🟢 table had **two rows
numbered 5** (the shipped `field_bool` row was inserted without renumbering), and the UI section
had a **duplicated orphan bullet** — the first line of the `UiGrid`/`UiFlow` spacing entry
appearing twice with no body, from my reconciliation of the U8c/U8e backlog merge conflict. Both
are the same class: a hand edit to a 2300-line document, verified by reading the diff rather than
the result. Neither is caught by any checker — `check_record_anchors` reads links, and
`check_next_steps_claims` reads only the handoff's State table.

**The human-time item is now the largest pending thing, and it is one pass, not three.** Three
separate bullets ask for the same scarce resource: the uncovered "GLOW" label, U8c's flow block
in its new upper-centre place, and the `field_bool` checkbox. They are consolidated in the
preamble rather than given a tier row, because the tiers rank work to schedule. The sharpest
single check among them is **toggling `visible` on a selected `UiPanel` and watching it vanish**:
that is what U8b shipped and could not drive, and no test or gate can reach it, because the
editor opens with nothing selected and `draw_inspector` returns at `"(nothing selected)"` before
any field is visited.

**The pass also retired a waiting condition, which is not a tier row but was the one
time-sensitive thing in the handoff.** The lavapipe CI trial (#307) was gated on ~10-20 `main`
runs accumulating. `git log 35a6f1e..main` is now 10, and both `Lavapipe device identity` and
`GPU tests under lavapipe` concluded `success` in all ten — checked per run against
`gh run view <id> --json jobs` rather than the run's overall conclusion, because a single
rolled-up `success` is a weaker claim than ten step-level ones, and confirmed against the
workflow having no `continue-on-error` that could mask a step failure. **Flakiness is
answered: zero in ten.** The cost half is a judgement on the principle and stays with the
human; the pass did not decide it.

**Predicted vs achieved.** Predicted 1-2 rankable rows from a window that was almost entirely one
feature arc plus docs; achieved 2, both about surfaces that are generated or untested rather than
about layout. The prediction that was wrong was a smaller one: I expected the window's declines to
need new "decided: no action" rows, and only one of the nine did — the rest are trigger-gated
*within* the UI section, where the trigger is "someone authors a scene that feels it", which is a
pending condition rather than a decision. Backlog 188,419 → 195,095 bytes (`wc -c`; the previous pass's "167 KB" figure was already stale by this window).

## The lavapipe CI trial is kept (2026-10-26)

**DECIDED 2026-10-26.** #307 shipped the GPU tests into CI on a Mesa-lavapipe software ICD
*as a trial*, with a gate of ~10-20 `main` runs and two questions the single green run could
not answer. Both are now answered, and the human's ruling was *"Leave the pixel assertions, 4
seconds is nothing."*

**Flakiness: zero in ten.** `git log 35a6f1e..main` is 10 commits since #307 merged. In all ten
runs both `Lavapipe device identity` and `GPU tests under lavapipe` concluded `success`. That was
checked **per run** with `gh run view <id> --json jobs`, not from the run's rolled-up conclusion,
and against the workflow having no `continue-on-error` that could mask a step failure — a single
`success` at run level is a weaker claim than ten at step level, and the difference matters here
because the whole point of the trial was stability rather than conformance.

**Cost: ~4 s of apt**, decided on the principle rather than on a measurement, because no
measurement can settle it: `main`'s own run-to-run spread is 83-121 s, an order of magnitude
larger than the change. This is the shape the trial's own record predicted, and the reason the
criteria said to judge it as a judgement.

**What the decision closes, and what it deliberately does not.** The trial *framing* is spent:
the two lavapipe steps are ordinary CI, and a red one is now a finding rather than a trial
result. It is **not** a commitment to the ICD pin — the `ubuntu-latest` re-check (Ubuntu 26
changes the Mesa version and may rename the manifest; the workflow globs `lvp_icd*.json`, untested
on 26) survives the decision untouched, because it is about the environment moving rather than
about whether these tests belong in CI. Revert stays cheap.

**Worth noting for the next trial.** A trial with a named gate and a written decision procedure
took one `gh` query and one judgement to close, two days after it shipped. The failure mode the
word "trial" exists to prevent — quiet permanence — was avoided by the gate being a *count of
runs* rather than a date: the count was reached by ordinary work, so the decision surfaced on its
own rather than needing someone to remember it.

## U11a — `layout_text` decodes UTF-8, and draws a fallback glyph

**SHIPPED 2026-10-26.** The first slice of
[U11](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-26-u11-text-quality-decomposition.md), taken in parallel
with U9 because the two share no files. `layout_text` decodes its `std::string` to codepoints
once and runs both passes over them; a missing *printable* codepoint resolves to `U+FFFD` if
the atlas has it, else `'?'`, else nothing; C0 controls and DEL keep the silent skip they
always had.

**The bug was worse than "a character is dropped".** Both the measure pass and the place pass
skipped unknown bytes, so `"Café"` lost the `é` *and was re-centred* as though it had always
been three characters — a silent layout shift, not just a missing glyph.

**The measurement the slice rests on, and the trap inside it.** The spec made cooking `U+FFFD`
conditional on the font providing it, and required that branch be measured rather than assumed.
It does **not**: `U+FFFD → glyph 0`, verified twice — by the slice with an stb probe, and by me
independently parsing the font's `cmap` format-4 subtable by hand (`'?' → 34`, `é → 171`,
`U+FFFD → 0`). Both fonts in the repo are the same file (`md5 e09c3d83…`), so one measurement
covers the engine and the tests.

**The trap:** `stbtt_FindGlyphIndex` returns 0 while stb still reports a **24×30 bbox with a
32.22 advance**, because it falls back to glyph 0 — and this font's `.notdef` is a *drawn box*.
A natural implementation gates the cook on "does it rasterise to something?", and that gate
would have baked `.notdef` **under the name `U+FFFD`**, reported that the font provides it, and
silently disabled the `'?'` leg forever. The slice gated on `FindGlyphIndex != 0` and said why
in the code. It then raised the aesthetic question separately — a box is the conventional
missing-character mark and `'?'` is indistinguishable from authored content — rather than
smuggling the answer in. That is exactly the right split, and the question is now a backlog row
waiting on one eye check.

**Verified by the supervisor rather than taken on report.** Suite **1536** and the mutation
re-run: reverting the decode to per-byte iteration fails `text_layout_test.cpp:119`
(`quads.size() == 4` → `5 == 4`) first, with `:141` and `:161` behind it, 21 cases / 18 passed
/ 3 failed. Renderer gate on the branch: exit 124, three `[INFO]`, zero VUID/ERROR/WARN.

**A correction the slice made to my own prediction, and it matters.** I predicted the count
assertion would fail as `3 == 4`. It fails as `5 == 4`, because `resolve_glyph` survives the
mutation and gives each of the two bytes its own `'?'`. The mutation isolates the decode
cleanly, which is what was asked — it simply does not reproduce the original bug's number, and
that is a property of the mutation rather than of the test. The slice also pointed out that the
ASCII no-op test **passes** under that mutation and is therefore carried by something else:
thirteen golden quads dumped at `%.9g` from the pre-change code and compared with `==`, not
`Approx`.

**One gap closed in review.** The resolution order's *first* leg — `U+FFFD` when present — was
executed by no test, since no available font has the glyph. The slice deferred it behind a
second binary TTF fixture. A fixture is not needed: `resolve_glyph` takes a `FontAtlas`, which
is a plain struct, so the branch is reachable by building one by hand. Added
`resolve_glyph prefers U+FFFD over '?' when the atlas has it`, with a **negative control** —
swapping the two legs fails it at `text_layout_test.cpp:249`. Suite **1536 → 1537**. The
general lesson is worth keeping: *"we need a binary fixture to test this"* is often false when
the function under test takes a plain struct.

**The cook change does reach the engine, which was worth confirming.** The font is cooked at
runtime from the `.ttf` on every launch (`demo_scene.hpp:177` → `read_ttf`); no cooked artefact
is committed anywhere (`git ls-files` finds only the two `.ttf` sources). So there is no re-cook
step for anyone to forget. A separate *project import* path does produce a `.font` blob
(`asset_io.hpp:87`), which would hold a stale copy until re-imported — irrelevant today, since
the cooked set is unchanged for this font, but it is the mechanism that will bite the
range-widening item, and it is noted there.

**Documented rather than left to be rediscovered:** the number of replacement marks per
ill-formed sequence is **not uniform**. `0xC0 0xAF` yields two (the lead byte alone proves the
overlong, orphaning its continuation); a surrogate yields one (nothing is wrong until the value
is decoded). That is fewer marks than Unicode 3.9's maximal-subpart recommendation, which is
what the spec asked for, and `utf8.hpp` now records it with the exact change that would make it
strict.

**Still unproven.** That a replacement mark *looks* like an error rather than content — an eye
check, now a backlog row. And nothing about HUD text as actually rendered: the pixel-invariance
argument is a derivation from an unchanged atlas and byte-identical ASCII geometry, not an
observation of pixels.
## U9a — an optional clip sub-rect on the three UI draw calls

**SHIPPED 2026-10-26.** The first slice of [U9](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-26-u9-clipping-scroll-decomposition.md).
`draw_ui`, `draw_ui_image` and `draw_ui_text` take a `UiClip = std::optional<Rect>`
defaulting to `std::nullopt`, and the Vulkan backend narrows the scissor around the draws
that carry one. No UI-side behaviour: nothing in `engine/ui/` or `app/run/demo_scene.hpp`
changed, and `RenderSystem` passes no clip yet.

**The decomposition's estimate was right, and for the stated reason.** Dynamic scissor state
was already plumbed at four sites, so this was interface surface plus a per-draw set: the
backend change is 55 lines, most of it comment. The slice's *test* surface was what overshot
the prediction, not its implementation.

**The open question the decomposition could not answer, answered.** "Which pass do the UI
draws execute in?" — **both.** All three begin with `ensure_destination_pass()`, which opens
the viewport pass when the editor has requested one and the swapchain pass otherwise, and each
then selects `viewport_pass_open_ ? vp_ui_pipeline_ : ui_pipeline_`. Those two destinations
differ in size in the editor (a swapchain-sized scene target against whatever the user dragged
the panel to), so the clip resolves through `ui_destination_extent()` — the **same selector**
`draw_hdr_resolve` already uses for its `inv_dest` push constant, whose comment says "one
question answered once". Also load-bearing and not in the premises: the UI draws record
immediately into `cmd_[frame_]`, unlike `draw_world_text`, whose vertices are consumed at
submit. A per-call scissor is only correct because of that.

**Two mutations, both re-run by the supervisor rather than taken on report.**
`set_ui_clip`'s `vkCmdSetScissor` deleted → one of ten GPU tests red at
`offscreen_ui_clip_test.cpp:98`, on *"below the clip, inside the quad, must be clear at
(64, 96)"*, expansion `126 <= 1`, with `actual` exactly the quad colour and `expected` exactly
the clear colour. That is the predicted assertion failing for the predicted reason.
`restore_ui_clip`'s deleted → one red, on *"unclipped draw after a clipped one covers the
bottom half"*, expansion `38 <= 1`.

**A supervisor premise the slice disproved, with evidence.** I had asked for two draws with
*different* clips in the same frame, reasoning that it would catch a missing restore. It does
not: a clipped draw calls `set_ui_clip`, which **overwrites** a leaked scissor rather than
intersecting with it, so a clipped draw can never be a leak's victim. Only a draw passing
`nullopt` — which records no scissor of its own — is vulnerable. The two-clip case tests
per-call clip *selection*; the trailing-unclipped case is what guards the restore. The ruling
changed and the test comment records which sub-case covers what.

**One judgement call, ruled and kept: `modules/render_vulkan/vulkan_ui_clip.{hpp,cpp}`.** The
normalized→pixel clamp is extracted as a pure helper the decomposition did not ask for, adding
8 unit tests. Kept, because the clamp's failure mode is *a scissor outside the render area* —
a validation error, not a wrong pixel — and nothing in the unit suite creates a `VkDevice`, so
logic buried in the backend is unreachable from any test. `vulkan_bloom_levels.hpp` and
`vulkan_swapchain_support.hpp` are the standing precedent and say exactly this in their own
headers. Spelled in plain `int32_t`/`uint32_t` rather than `VkRect2D` so it needs no
`vulkan.h`.

**Three things this slice does NOT establish, kept as the weaker sentence.**
1. **The editor-viewport branch of the extent selector is covered by nothing.** Writing
   `extent_` unconditionally would leave all 1529 unit tests and all 10 GPU tests green while
   every clip in the editor's panel resolved against the wrong extent. The selector was chosen
   by following `draw_hdr_resolve`'s precedent, not by measuring. Backlogged with the viewport
   readback seam it depends on.
2. **`REQUIRE(validation_messages.empty())` is vacuous** wherever the layer is absent, and
   nothing reports whether it was enabled. The slice's validation evidence is the renderer gate
   (exit 124, three `[INFO]`, zero VUIDs, byte-identical before and after) and the unit-tested
   clamp — not that assertion. Backlogged. **Its own count was wrong when first written**: the
   entry said "five GPU tests", and `grep -n 'validation_messages.empty()' tests/gpu/*.cpp`
   gives **eight sites across seven of the ten**. Corrected in review. Third instance this week
   of a count narrower than the claim built on it.
3. **The pixel-edge rounding policy** (round-to-nearest against round-outward) is untested by
   construction: U9a's clip edges land on whole pixels (0.5 × 128 = 64), so its assertions
   cannot distinguish them. Deferred to U9d, which is the first time an edge will land on a
   fractional pixel.

**Counts.** Unit **1520 → 1529** (8 clamp cases, 1 `RenderSystem` case pinning that it passes
*no* clip on any of the three draws — worth a case precisely because the parameter is
defaulted, so a stray clip would compile silently). GPU **8 → 10**.

## U9b — `UiClipRect`: intersect down the hierarchy, for drawing AND hit-testing

**SHIPPED 2026-10-26.** The second slice of [U9](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-26-u9-clipping-scroll-decomposition.md).
A `UiClipRect` marker component on a container clips its whole subtree to that container's
resolved rect; `resolve_clip` folds every clipping ancestor's rect by intersection;
`RenderSystem`'s three HUD passes carry the result into U9a's parameter; and
`button_hit_candidates` drops a candidate wholly outside its clip and trims one partly
outside. Draw and hit in one slice, as the decomposition required.

**The name collision was settled by renaming the new thing.** `UiClip` was already taken as
U9a's device-side alias (`RenderDevice::UiClip`, an `std::optional<Rect>`), and
`render_system.hpp` now mentions both within a few lines. The component is
`mosaic::engine::ui::UiClipRect` — chosen because it states the semantics rather than
restating the taken word: the subtree is clipped to *this entity's rect*. U9a's alias is
untouched; it is shipped and referenced in this record. Both names are cross-referenced in
`ui_clip_rect.hpp`'s header comment, which opens by saying which "clip" is which.

**Three briefed premises. Two held; one was wrong, and one of the two that held needed a
correction to stay true.**

1. **"A clip must change neither position nor size, so it is a separate walk, not a second
   `slot_for_child`." — HOLDS, and is implemented that way.** `resolve_clip` is an iterative
   walk up `UiParent` with the same `kMaxDepth = 16` guard as `resolve_parent_frame` and
   `inherited_alpha`, returning an intersected rect and touching no frame.
2. **"The HUD panel pass makes exactly one `draw_ui` call per frame after a stable
   order-sort." — HOLDS** (`render_system.hpp:536-542` before this slice). **But the other
   half of the same premise was WRONG: the image and text passes do NOT group by texture.**
   The decomposition says "the HUD pass already groups `draw_ui_image` and `draw_ui_text`
   calls by texture … a clip is one more grouping key". They do not group or merge anything:
   each emits **one call per element**, carrying that element's own texture. So the clip
   needed no grouping key there at all — it is one more field per call — and the only pass
   with a batching question was the panel pass. Recorded in the backlog so a later slice that
   *does* merge by texture knows it must make the clip a second key of that merge.
3. **"A `clip_resolves` counter is a non-goal; say so if the existing counters become
   misleading." — THEY DO NOT, and it is held there by construction rather than by luck.**
   `resolve_clip` probes each ancestor for `UiClipRect` and calls `resolve_ui_rect` **only**
   for one that carries it, so a scene with no clip anywhere — which is every scene shipped
   today, including the demo scene — spends zero extra `resolves`, `scans`, `visits`,
   `container_probes` and `allocations`. That is asserted directly ("a clip-free scene pays
   no layout resolves for the clip walk") with the converse in the same case, so it is a
   measurement and not a function that never runs. `ui_layout_cost_test.cpp`'s exact counts
   (32/128/512 resolves, 48/192/768 probes) are untouched. A clipping scene's clip resolves
   are counted as `resolves`, which is correct: they are genuine `resolve_parent_frame`
   entries. No counter was added.

**HOW DRAW ORDER IS PRESERVED ACROSS CLIP GROUPS, which is the slice's one subtle thing.**
The panel pass collects `(order, quad, clip)`, stable-sorts by `order` as before, and then
emits **runs**: a new `draw_ui` call begins wherever the next panel's clip differs from the
previous one's. It is a **run-length grouping, never a group-by**. For panels A(order 1,
clip X), B(2, Y), C(3, X), a group-by emits `[A, C]` then `[B]` — which draws C *before* B
although C has the higher order, so B lands on top of C. The run-length form emits `[A]`,
`[B]`, `[C]`: three calls instead of two, in exactly the authored z-order. The sort is the
contract; batching may only ever merge **neighbours** in it.

That is **verified rather than assumed**, by a case that asserts the *sequence* and not only
the count — `RenderSystem preserves draw order when two clips INTERLEAVE in the sort` — with
each quad given a distinct width so the recorded calls are identifiable, plus the property
stated as a pairwise inequality across consecutive calls. A count-only assertion would have
read as a performance detail; a group-by gets the count wrong in the *other* direction (two
calls, not three), so the count alone is ambiguous about which bug it caught.

The cost is stated rather than hidden: interleaved clips cost one call per run, so a scene
alternating clips between every panel gets one call per panel. Authoring contiguous `order`
ranges per clip region collapses it back to one call per region. Backlogged as a 🟢 row with
the condition that would make it worth fixing.

**A property the obvious implementation gets wrong by one ULP, found by a test rather than by
reading.** "A clip changes **neither** the position nor the size of what it clips" is the
sentence that distinguishes a clip from `resolve_parent_frame`'s frame narrowing —
`RenderDevice::UiClip` says it in those words. The first version of
`a button fully inside its clip keeps exactly its unclipped rect` **failed**, printing
`0.2f == 0.2f`: `intersect_rects` recomputes an extent as `min(a.x+a.w, b.x+b.w) - max(a.x,
b.x)`, which for a contained rect is `(x + w) - x` — and in float that is not always `w`. The
fix is `clip_rect(rect, clip)`, which returns `rect` untouched when the clip already contains
it and intersects otherwise. One ULP hits nothing a user can see; the reason it was fixed
rather than asserted away is that the sentence above then needs no footnote, and the draw path
and the hit path keep agreeing **by construction** for every element a clip does not actually
trim. The same fast path, in the argument order `clip_rect(accumulated, ancestor)`, makes a
nested clip contained in its ancestor resolve bit-identically. Both are asserted, including
the negative control that the naive intersection *does* drift.

**Three mutations, each targeting a different mechanism, so the three halves are shown to
fail independently.**

1. **The decisive one — replace the intersection with "use the innermost clip"**
   (`if (!clip.has_value()) { clip = ancestor; }`). **Five unit tests and both new GPU tests
   red.** Named failures: `ui_clip_rect_test.cpp:221`
   `REQUIRE(clip->w == Approx(0.25f))`, expansion `0.5f == Approx( 0.25 )`;
   `render_system_test.cpp:3539` `REQUIRE(device.ui_clips[0]->w == Approx(0.25f))`, expansion
   `0.5f == Approx( 0.25 )`; `ui_button_rects_test.cpp:322`
   `REQUIRE(button_hit_candidates(world).empty())`, expansion `false`. The GPU test failed at
   `offscreen_ui_clip_hierarchy_test.cpp:161` on its normalized-space pin
   (`0.5f == 0.25f`) **before** reaching a pixel — so that pin was bypassed and the run
   repeated, which put the failure on the **predicted pixel**: line 69,
   `126 <= 1`, message *"inside inner but right of outer, must be clear -- the
   innermost-only bug at (80, 64) actual=(225, 188, 99, 255) expected=(99, 137, 188, 255)"* —
   the quad colour where the clear colour belongs.
2. **The hit half alone — delete the clip filter in `button_hit_candidates`, leaving the draw
   path intact.** **Four tests red, all four in the hit path and none in the draw path** --
   `a button wholly outside its clip produces NO hit candidate`, `a button partly outside its
   clip reports only the surviving rect`, `the hit test intersects NESTED clips rather than
   taking the innermost` and `a clipped-away button lets the press reach what is beneath it`.
   That separation is the independence the one-slice decision rests on: the draw half stayed
   green while every hit assertion went red.
3. **Draw order — replace the run-length emit with a group-by** (bucket every quad sharing a
   clip, buckets emitted in first-seen order). **Exactly one test red, and it is the
   interleave case** — but the first version of that case failed on
   `REQUIRE(device.ui_draws.size() == 3)`, expansion `2 == 3`, and a count alone does not say
   the HUD was **reordered**, only that the batching changed. Two things came out of chasing
   that, and both changed the test rather than the finding.
   **(a)** Bypassing the count assertions did not reveal the ordering failure; it turned it
   into a **SIGABRT**, because a group-by emits two calls and the ordering assertion was
   written as `ui_draws[0][0] < ui_draws[1][0] < ui_draws[2][0]`, which reads past the end.
   The count assertions were load-bearing, not decoration.
   **(b)** The ordering property is now asserted as a **flatten over every quad across every
   call in call order** — the sequence the GPU actually rasterises — and is asserted
   **before** the counts, because a `REQUIRE` on a count aborts the case before any ordering
   assertion is reached. Re-run against the same mutant, the first failure is
   `render_system_test.cpp:3383`, `REQUIRE(drawn_widths[1] < drawn_widths[2])`, expansion
   `0.15f < 0.1f`, with the message *"flattened draw order (widths): 0.05, 0.15, 0.1"* — C
   drawn before B, named as such. That is the assertion a future group-by meets first.

**The renderer gate: byte-identical, and it exercises nothing new — stated plainly rather
than rounded up.** `timeout 12 env MOSAIC_VK_SYNC_VALIDATION=1 ./build/debug/app/mosaic
editor` gives exit **124**, three `[INFO]` lines, zero VUIDs/ERROR/WARN, and `diff` against
the capture on unmodified `main` is empty on both streams. **That proves no regression and
nothing about the clip**, because **the demo scene authors no `UiClipRect`**, so every clip
in it resolves to `nullopt` and the HUD emits exactly the command stream it emitted before.
Authoring one was declined for this slice: the obvious candidate is the `hud_stack`, whose own
comment stakes out "the three children must EXACTLY tile the container — no seam, no overlap,
at any window size", and a clip whose pixel edge rounds differently from the children's quads
could open a one-pixel seam that **no test can see** (the editor path is invisible to the gate,
and the fractional-edge rounding policy is U9a's own deferred item). The decomposition already
puts the first authored region at U9d, where a human looks at it. Backlogged.

**What this slice does NOT establish.**
1. **No authored clip exists anywhere, so the whole feature is unexercised outside tests.**
   The engine path is covered (38 unit tests, 2 GPU tests); the *authored* path — a clip in a
   real scene, in the editor, at a real window size — is covered by nothing, and the renderer
   gate above cannot stand in for it.
2. **The editor-viewport branch of the extent selector is still covered by nothing**, exactly
   as U9a recorded. U9b did not touch `ui_destination_extent()` and inherits that blind spot
   unchanged: a clip resolved in the editor's viewport panel resolves against `vp_extent_`,
   and only the `extent_` path is exercised.
3. **`find_order_disagreements` does not inherit the clip filter.** `find_order_conflicts`
   does, for free, because it reads `button_hit_candidates`; the other one reads
   `for_each<UiButton>` directly and will therefore report findings about buttons that are
   clipped away. Named as a non-goal by the decomposition and already in the backlog as the
   open 🟢 decision U8b left; U9b makes it concrete rather than hypothetical.
4. **`REQUIRE(validation_messages.empty())` remains vacuous** wherever the layer is absent,
   and the two new GPU tests deliberately do not add a ninth and tenth site. The clip's
   validation evidence is the renderer gate.
5. **No fractional clip edge is tested.** Every edge in both GPU tests lands on a whole pixel
   (0.25 × 128 = 32, 0.5 → 64, 0.75 → 96) on purpose, so no assertion here depends on how a
   partially covered edge pixel rounds. Still U9d's.

**Counts.** Unit **1546 → 1584** (+38). GPU **10 → 12**. Suite wall 3.87 s → 4.06 s. One
CMake change beyond the test list: `mosaic_gpu_tests` now links `mosaic_engine`, because the
new GPU test resolves its clip out of a real `World` rather than writing one down — which is
the only thing it adds over U9a's device-level test. `mosaic_engine` is three translation
units over `mosaic_foundation`, so it costs that suite nothing it was not already linking.

## U9b's supervision pass: the premise I got wrong, and the mutation that earned its place

**2026-10-26.** Review notes on the slice above, kept separate from it because the findings are
about the brief rather than the work.

**My decomposition was wrong about the HUD passes, and the slice caught it by reading.** Both
the U9 spec and my brief said *"the image and text passes already group `draw_ui_image` /
`draw_ui_text` calls by texture, so a clip is one more grouping key there."* They do not group
anything. `render_system.hpp`'s image pass emits
`device.draw_ui_image(std::vector<UiImageQuad>{pr.second.first}, …)` — **one call per element,
with a single-element vector** — and the text pass emits one call per `UiText`. The sort
reorders *calls*, not quads within a call. Verified independently during review by reading both
loops rather than taking the report.

Consequence: only the **panel** pass had a batching question, and the clip was one more *field*
in the other two. The slice was smaller here than briefed.

**How the error was made, since it is the third of this shape in two days.** The spec cited
`render_system.hpp:588` and `:634` as evidence of grouping. Those two lines are the bodies of
the per-element emit loops. I read a line number, saw a `draw_ui_*` call inside a `for`, and
inferred a grouping that was never there — the same mistake as citing
`grep -ri scissor engine/render/` for a claim about the backend, and as the four denominator
errors across the U8 arc. **The pattern is not "wrong facts"; it is reading one line and
describing the loop around it.**

**The run-length point was worth briefing, and the proof of that is the test's own history.**
Grouping HUD quads by clip reorders the HUD: for panels A(clip X), B(clip Y), C(clip X) a
group-by draws A, C, B, so B lands on top of C although C has the higher `order`. The slice
implemented run-length batching — merge only *neighbours* in the sorted sequence — and I
re-ran the group-by mutation myself: exactly one test red, at
`render_system_test.cpp:3383`, expansion `0.15f < 0.1f`, message
*"flattened draw order (widths): 0.05, 0.15, 0.1"*. The failure names the reordering.

What makes that worth recording is what the slice had to do to get there. Its first version
caught the mutation on a **count** (`ui_draws.size() == 3`, `2 == 3`), which does not say the
HUD was reordered; bypassing the counts turned the failure into a **SIGABRT**, because the
ordering assertion indexed `ui_draws[2]` on a two-call result. The final form asserts the
property as a **flatten across all calls in call order**, placed *before* the counts. Three
arrangements of the same test, only the third of which reports the actual defect.

**A correctness property found by a failing test, not by design.** `intersect_rects` recomputes
an extent as `min(a.x+a.w, b.x+b.w) - max(a.x, b.x)`, which for a rect already inside the clip
is `(x + w) - x` — and in float that is not always `w`. The case *"a button fully inside its
clip keeps exactly its unclipped rect"* failed with `0.2f == 0.2f`. The slice fixed the
implementation rather than weakening the test to `Approx`, which was the right call: *"a clip
changes neither the position nor the size of what it clips"* is the sentence that distinguishes
a clip from frame narrowing, and a sentence that needs a footnote is a weaker contract. It also
keeps the draw and hit paths agreeing **by construction** wherever a clip trims nothing.

**Three judgement calls, ruled.**
1. **Component named `UiClipRect`**, not `UiClip` — accepted. `RenderDevice::UiClip` is the
   shipped device alias and `render_system.hpp` mentions both in one function.
2. **No authored clip in the demo scene** — accepted, and its reasoning is better than mine.
   The obvious candidate is `hud_stack`, whose own comment requires its three children to tile
   it exactly at any window size; a clip edge rounding differently from the children's quads
   could open a one-pixel seam that **nothing can observe**, since the editor path is invisible
   to the gate and the rounding policy is deferred to U9d. The consequence is stated plainly
   rather than hidden: **the renderer gate is vacuous for this slice** — byte-identical before
   and after, because no clip is authored, so it proves no regression and nothing about the
   clip.
3. **`same_clip` compares floats exactly** — accepted. It can only err by failing to merge,
   never by merging wrongly, so the cost is a draw call and not a wrong pixel.

**Counts.** Unit **1546 → 1584**, GPU **10 → 12**, all re-run by me, with the stale-binary trap
hit and recovered: restoring the mutated header and rebuilding only `build/debug` left the GPU
binary mutant, and `ctest -L gpu` reported 2 failures until `build/gputests` was rebuilt too.
That is the `mv`-mtime landmine wearing a second face — two build directories, one restore.

**A sandbox fact worth keeping.** A fresh `-DMOSAIC_ENABLE_GPU_TESTS=ON` build dir cannot
configure here: FetchContent re-clones every dependency and `git clone` fails with
`could not lock config file … enet-src/.git/config`. The workaround is to point the new dir at
the already-populated sources with `-DFETCHCONTENT_SOURCE_DIR_<DEP>=…/build/debug/_deps/<dep>-src`
for each dependency. Nothing in the tree depends on it and CI clones normally.

**U9b's CI failure, and what it says about the local gates.** The PR went red on the *Unit
tests* step with **all 1584 tests passing**. The failure was the step's guard, not the suite:
`check_ctest_run: O1: 1 duplicate test names in the JUnit file`, with `cases=1583` against 1584
tests run. `tests/unit/ui/ui_clip_rect_test.cpp` had reused
*"a UiParent cycle terminates instead of recursing forever"*, which `ui_hover_style_test.cpp:232`
already owned — an entirely reasonable name for the same guard in a second place. Renamed to
*"resolve_clip terminates on a UiParent cycle instead of recursing forever"*.

**The interesting part is that no local gate sees it.** `ctest` runs both cases happily and
reports 1584 passing; `check_test_case_names.py` lints the leading-`--`/bracket/comma rules and
not uniqueness. Only the JUnit file collapses two identically-named cases into one entry, and
only CI builds a JUnit file. The handoff's `TEST_CASE` landmine now carries the duplicate rule
**and the two commands that reproduce the check locally**, because the failure costs a full CI
round trip otherwise, and reads as "the suite failed" when the suite passed.

**A false positive of my own, worth recording beside it.** My first duplicate hunt was
`ctest -N | sed 's/.*: //' | sort | uniq -d`, which reported *two* duplicates. The second —
*"every global has a non-empty docstring"* — was an artefact of my own `sed`: the real names are
`install_world_globals: every global…` and `install_imgui_globals: every global…`, and stripping
to the last `": "` deleted exactly the part that distinguishes them. CI had said **1**
duplicate, so the disagreement was the signal. A one-liner that mangles its input is the same
class of error as the checker that reported 14 dead anchors that were all alive.

## U11b — multi-line and word-wrap

**2026-10-27, branch `feat/u11b-wrap`.** The second and last slice of the
[U11 decomposition](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-26-u11-text-quality-decomposition.md), on the
[plan](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-27-u11b-wrap-plan.md) that carries the oracle. `layout_text`
now honours `\n` and wraps at a `UiText::wrap_width`, and anchoring resolves **per line**.

**The oracle, which was written before anything was measured and then earned its keep twice.**
"Each line of a block must lay out, in X, exactly as if that line had been the whole single-line
string." It is one assertion rather than two because it is only satisfiable when the measure
pass and the place pass agree *per line*: a line measured at one width and placed at another is
anchored at `centre - w_measured/2` and inked over `w_placed`, so its glyphs move. It is an
X-axis claim deliberately — Y legitimately differs, by `i x line_height` and by the whole box
moving when a centred or bottom-anchored block gains a line.

**What makes the invariant hold by construction, not by care.** `break_lines` is the *only*
place a line is measured: it returns `{begin, end, advance}` ranges over the decoded codepoint
vector, and the place pass reads those advances rather than re-accumulating them. A second
accumulation loop is exactly how the per-line version of U11a's invariant would be lost.

**The long-word policy, which the spec's gate constrained but did not decide.** A word longer
than the wrap width with no space to break at: **forced break between codepoints**, not
overflow. CSS's default is overflow and it was the tempting answer; it is wrong *here* because
U9b shipped `UiClipRect` two commits earlier, so an overflowing label is now **masked** rather
than merely ugly — overflow would make an authored wrap width silently lose text. The second
reason is that overflow would make the slice's own gate 1 vacuous: if no break happens for a
no-space string, a per-byte implementation is indistinguishable from a per-codepoint one on
exactly the input chosen to tell them apart. At least one codepoint is always placed per line,
or a glyph wider than the whole wrap width closes an empty line forever.

**Two bugs the oracle caught, one of them mine and one of them the code's.**
1. *In the test harness.* `wrap_fraction` converted atlas pixels to a `wrap_width` and dropped
   `frame.h`, so a nested-frame wrap width came out `1/0.59` too large and the text never
   wrapped at all. The oracle failed with `0.24397 == Approx(0.1782)` — the second word still
   sitting on the first line. A left-anchored-only test suite would have passed it.
2. *In `break_lines`, found by tracing rather than by a failure.* The soft-break candidate was
   "the last space seen", which for `"AAA   AAA"` closes the line at `"AAA "` — a trailing
   space inside the line's *measured* advance. Invisible for a left-anchored line and a visible
   half-space shift for a centred one. The candidate is now **the first space of the run**, and
   `"AAA AAA"` and `"AAA   AAA"` wrap to byte-identical quads.

**Mutation 1, the decisive one: anchor every line against the widest line's width.**
`pen_x = box.x` for every line, dropping the `(widest - ln.advance) * sx * align` offset.
Predicted: only the oracle fails, at `text_layout_wrap_test.cpp:133`, first on
`"I\nWWWWWW"` / `Anchor::Top` / quad 0, block x smaller than alone x. Achieved: exactly that —
`REQUIRE(block[at + i].rect.x == Approx(alone[i].rect.x).margin(1e-7))` with
`0.30349f == Approx(0.3812059462)`, 29 assertions passing first, which places it at block 0,
the *second* anchor in the list. **1 of 1594 tests failed, and it is the only test in the slice
that is not left-anchored.** Every other new case uses `TopLeft` or `BottomLeft`, where the
alignment band is 0 and the offset is zero, so every one of them passes on broken code. That is
the whole argument for enumerating all nine anchors in the oracle rather than picking one.

**Mutation 2, the mid-character break:** replace `decode_utf8(text)` with a per-byte expansion,
which is the only way a break *can* land inside a character — there is no byte notion inside
`break_lines`. Predicted: `REQUIRE(qm.size() == 8)` fails with 16. Achieved: exactly that, at
`text_layout_wrap_test.cpp:169`, `16 == 8`. Four tests went red, not one: U11a's three
replacement-glyph cases fail too, which is correct rather than noise — gate 1 is *"the U11a
dependency made observable"*, so a mutation that removes the dependency must take U11a's own
gates with it. Reported as not exclusive to the new test.

**Gate 5 — byte-identical quads for today's labels — was proved twice, not asserted.** Before
any edit, a temporary dump case emitted 60 quads at `%.9g` (which round-trips a float exactly)
over 8 cases: both strings `app/run/demo_scene.hpp` actually authors (`"HP"` and `"MOSAIC"`, at
their authored anchor/pos/height), the screen frame and a nested one, and all three horizontal
anchor bands. After the change the same dump `diff`s **clean, 62 lines, zero differences**. Four
of the eight cases are now a permanent test comparing those pre-change literals with `==`, not
`Approx`. Structurally it is exact rather than lucky: for one line the per-line offset is
`(widest - ln.advance)` with `widest == ln.advance`, i.e. literally `0.0f`; `box_h` is
`1.0f * height`; and the offset is taken against the *measured* `widest` rather than against
`box.w`, which `resolve_in` produced as `(w/frame.w)*frame.w` and which is **not**
bit-identically `w` for a frame width that is not a power of two.

**A premise in the brief was stale, in the way the house failure mode predicts.**
`layout_text`'s non-test callers were given as `world_text.hpp:25` and
`render_system.hpp:614`. The first is right; the second is **`render_system.hpp:704`** — U9b
rewrote that file on 2026-10-26 and moved the call 90 lines down. The *claim* survived
(`git grep` over all tracked files finds no third non-test caller; `font_cook.cpp`,
`ui_hierarchy.hpp` and `ui_layout.hpp` mention it only in comments), but the line number did
not, which is what reading one line rather than the loop around it produces.

**The parameter was added so that one caller changed, not two.** `wrap_width` is a *trailing
defaulted* parameter after `frame`, so `world_text.hpp` is untouched and `render_system.hpp`
changes only to forward the new field. `UiText::wrap_width` is a fraction of the **parent
frame's width**, matching `pos` and mirroring `height`'s relationship to the frame's height, so
a wrapped label rescales with its container instead of keeping a screen-absolute width. Both
`UiText` aggregate initialisers in `demo_scene.hpp` are designated, so the
positional-brace-init hazard did not fire; the field went in beside `height` and the reflection
round-trip test now sets it to a non-default `0.42f`.

**Numbers.** Suite 1584 -> **1595** (10 new `[wrap]` cases carrying 1234 assertions, plus the
billboard line-count case added by the supervision pass), all passing, 4.4 s local. The JUnit
duplicate-name guard reproduces clean (`check_ctest_run: OK, 1595 of 1595`). The renderer gate was run even though gate 5 says the
emitted quads cannot have changed: `timeout 12 env MOSAIC_VK_SYNC_VALIDATION=1 ./build/debug/app/mosaic editor`
exits **124** with three `[INFO]` lines and zero VUID/ERROR/WARN.

**The slice is NOT screen-space only, which I got wrong and supervision caught.** `'\n'` became
a line break **unconditionally** — `break_lines` splits at it whatever the wrap width is, and
the wrap flag gates only the *width-based* break — so it reached `build_world_text`, the
billboard path, through the trailing defaulted `wrap_width` I added specifically so that caller
would not have to be touched. **`world_text.hpp` is untouched in this diff and its behaviour
changed.** Measured on both sides rather than read: `build_world_text` over `"AB"` gives 2
distinct vertex Y rows and over `"A\nB"` gives 4, i.e. two lines; and before U11b `'\n'` was
`< 0x20`, so `resolve_glyph` returned `nullptr` (`text_layout.hpp:33` on `main`) and the place
loop's `continue` dropped it at `:82` — genuinely one line.

That matters because `build_world_text` centres vertically on `cy0 = 0.5f`, *"a local box ~1
line tall"*, so a multi-line `WorldLabel` hangs below its anchor by half the extra block
height. It is live, not conditional; it is unobservable only because no authored `WorldLabel`
contains a newline. The backlog entry originally said the billboard path "still renders as one
line" — a right deferral with a false reason, which would have told the next reader the bug
could not exist and stopped them looking. **The lesson is about the mechanism, not the typo:** a
*trailing defaulted parameter* is the one shape of change that alters an untouched caller's
behaviour while leaving no trace in the diff, and the saving in call-site churn is paid for by
losing the diff as an index of what changed. Next time, enumerate the callers' behaviour, not
just their signatures. `tests/unit/ui/world_text_test.cpp` now pins the line-count fact and
**deliberately nothing more** — pinning the centring would make the arithmetic harder to fix
while it still waits on a design decision.

**What nobody has looked at.** No multi-line label is authored anywhere, so **no human has ever
seen wrapped text in this engine** — on either path. The suite proves the arithmetic and says
nothing about whether a wrapped HUD label reads well; that is an eye check, and it joins the
three already owed.
## U9c — a wheel delta on `PointerState`, and both providers (2026-10-27)

**Spec:** [per-slice plan](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-27-u9c-pointer-wheel.md), under the
[U9 decomposition](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-26-u9-clipping-scroll-decomposition.md).
**Ships the field and both providers and no consumer** — `UiScroll` is U9d.

**The design question was never the field; it was who resets the accumulator.** GLFW's scroll
is event-driven only (there is no `glfwGetScroll`), so an accumulator is forced. The
constraint that settles where it resets was handed to me as a premise and held under checking:
**the pointer provider is invoked twice per tick** — `UiSystem::update`
(`engine/ui/ui_system.hpp:51`) and `ClickNudgeSystem::update` (`app/run/click_nudge.hpp:49`)
share one `glfw_pointer` lambda, and `app/main.cpp` says in words that they must agree about
the pointer within a tick, because the UI-claim arbitration rests on it. So the obvious design
— reading the accumulator takes its contents — hands the second caller `0`, makes the two
systems disagree inside one tick, and does it with every test green, because no test before
this slice called the provider twice without a tick between.

**Checking that premise turned up a stronger version of it.** It is not "once per frame"
either. `FramePump::run` calls `loop_.advance(world, dt)` and *then* `on_frame`
(`app/run/frame_pump.hpp:64-72`), and `on_frame` is where `glfwPollEvents()` lives — so a
frame's scroll events are seen by the *next* frame's ticks. And `RuntimeLoop::advance`
(`engine/runtime/runtime_loop.hpp:27`) is a fixed-timestep accumulator capped at
`max_steps_per_advance = 8`, so one frame can run **zero to eight** ticks. Sixteen reads of the
same delta in one frame is a legal schedule.

**Where it landed.** `mosaic::app::WheelAccumulator` in `app/run/window_pointer.hpp`: `add`
from the GLFW scroll callback, `value()` `const` so it cannot become consuming by accident, and
`reset()` called by the frame loop at the **top of `on_frame`, immediately before
`glfwPollEvents()`**. All three positions are load-bearing: reset after the poll discards every
event the poll just delivered (the wheel never works at all, suite green); reset from the
reader breaks the two-callers rule. The editor needed no accumulator — `ImGui::GetIO().MouseWheel`
is already per-frame accumulated and reset in `NewFrame`, and `ViewportPanel::pointer_state` is
`const`, so the no-consume property is free on that side.

**Two wrinkles. One deferred, one closed here on the coordinator's ruling.** Every tick of one
`advance` sees the same delta, so a catch-up frame could show it to eight ticks — deferred,
because the right answer depends on what *applying* a delta means and that is U9d's decision.
The other — a frame that runs zero ticks clearing a delta nothing observed — was offered as
deferred and **the coordinator took it into U9c instead**, with reasoning worth keeping:

- **The frequency is not marginal.** `options.hpp:45-46` defaults `fps = 60.0` and
  `fixed_hz = 60.0`, and `frame_pump.hpp` paces to `1.0 / fps`, so the banked remainder sits
  right at `fixed_dt` and **ordinary timing jitter alone** produces zero-tick frames. Worse,
  `--fps` is user-facing: any value above `--fixed-hz` makes zero-tick frames the **majority**,
  so the fraction of notches dropped would be set by an unrelated CLI flag.
- **Who pays.** U9d adds the first consumer *and* carries the human's eye check on a real
  scroll. Handing it an input path that drops notches at a jitter-determined rate makes that
  check ambiguous in the one way an eye check must not be: "scrolling feels like it stutters"
  would be indistinguishable from a bug in U9d's own offset arithmetic. The human's attention
  is the scarcest input in the loop.

**So the clear is gated on `RuntimeLoop::tick_count()` having moved**, read in `on_frame` after
`advance` — the only place that sees whether a tick actually ran. There is deliberately **no
unconditional `reset()`** left on the class: an ungated clear is the lossy design the gate
exists to prevent, and leaving one callable would leave it reachable.

**The price of the gate, and the ruling on it.** A stretch of frames with no tick now
accumulates with nothing to stop it, which a low `--fixed-hz` makes reachable. The decision
taken: **saturate at `kMaxPendingWheel = 120.0f`**, keeping the sign, clamping the *sum* rather
than discarding events, so scrolling up then down still cancels one notch per notch. Letting it
grow instead would hand U9d's first consumer a delta whose magnitude encodes how long the sim
was stalled, which is not information about what the user did; discarding events instead would
reintroduce the dropped-notch loss the gate exists to fix.

**The number 120 is a chosen bound and is NOT measured — that distinction is deliberate.** No
wheel-rate measurement exists on this project and none was available here, so the value
satisfies a shape rather than deriving from data: large enough that no plausible real gesture
is clipped, small enough that a post-stall tick gets a jump a consumer can reason about. The
first draft of its comment called it "~two seconds of vigorous scrolling", which is an
unmeasured estimate written in the voice of a fact — the same defect class as a rationale that
reads as derived and is therefore either over-trusted or treated as unchangeable. Corrected to
say plainly that it is chosen, that no human has scrolled against it, and that **what would
settle it is a real scroll gesture on real hardware once U9d has a consumer** — a question for
U9d's eye pass. "Not shown to be wrong" and "proved right" are different sentences, and the
weaker one is the true one here. The SHAPE (saturate, sign-preserving, clamp the sum) is
argued, is ratified, and is asserted in both directions over 10,000 events.

**The oracle, written before anything was measured:** both callers within one tick observe the
same delta; if the second sees `0`, the read is consuming and the slice is wrong. Its complement
is the mutation gate below. "`ctest` is green" was not available as an oracle — the suite was
green before the field existed and is green with the field unread.

**A real bug this slice shipped, then found by reading, and then pinned.** The GLFW scroll
callback is a plain C function pointer with no captures, so the obvious way to reach a
stack-local accumulator is `glfwSetWindowUserPointer`. **That slot is already owned.**
`VulkanRenderDevice`'s constructor writes `this` into it
(`modules/render_vulkan/vulkan_render_device.cpp:364`) and its `framebuffer_resize_callback`
casts it straight back and dereferences it with **no null check** (`:2785`). The first draft of
this slice therefore made the first window resize of `mosaic run --window` write a bool through
a `WheelAccumulator*` reinterpreted as a `VulkanRenderDevice*`, and left the swapchain never
recreating.

**Nothing in this repo could have caught it, and the near miss is the lesson.** `ctest` creates
no `VkDevice`; the renderer gate runs `mosaic editor`, a *different* code path that installs no
scroll callback and whose log was byte-identical before and after; and the windowed smoke run
under `timeout` never resized the window. All three gates were green on broken code. It was
found by grepping `glfwSetWindowUserPointer` across the tree while checking an unrelated
question about the stopped editor — i.e. by accident. The fix is a file-scope `g_wheel` beside
the existing `g_stop`, plus a textual pin forbidding `glfwSetWindowUserPointer` in `app/main.cpp`
with the reason attached. The backend half — the missing null check, and nothing anywhere
declaring that the device owns that slot — is a Rendering-section backlog entry, since this
slice was not scoped to touch the backend.

**The stopped editor, checked because a `tick_count`-gated clear could have interacted with
it.** It does not, and the reason is worth stating because the conclusion is right for a
different mechanism than the obvious one. `app/run/editor.cpp:296` calls `loop.stop()` every
frame while stopped and `RuntimeLoop::advance` returns 0 **without touching `tick_count_`**
while stopped, so in a stopped editor every frame is a zero-tick frame, permanently. But the
editor path uses `ImGui::GetIO().MouseWheel` and **no `WheelAccumulator` at all** — the
accumulator and its gate exist only on `app/main.cpp`'s `mosaic run --window` path, where
`FramePump` calls `loop_.start()` and ticks advance — so the gate is unreachable there and
cannot interact with the stopped loop. The *effect* ("the wheel does nothing in a stopped
editor") is correct, and correct for the right reason: not because the provider suppresses the
wheel — it does not, `pointer_state` gates on `hovered` only, deliberately — but because **no
tick runs, so nothing calls the provider at all.** That is the same mechanism
`editor_play_policy.hpp` already documents for `--ticks`, and the same one that makes the
`should_start_playing` exception necessary.

**Four mutations. Three predicted exactly; the fourth exposed a gap in how I enumerate
predictions.**

1. **`WheelAccumulator::reset()` emptied** (the per-frame reset dropped). Predicted: the final
   `REQUIRE(tick_reads() == Approx(0.0f))` of *"the frame loop, not the reader, is what ends a
   wheel delta's life"*, expected `0.0`, actual `3.0`; plus the post-`reset` assertion of
   *"WheelAccumulator sums every scroll event between two resets"*, expected `0.0`, actual
   `2.5`. **Both failed, exactly those two, exactly those values**
   (`tests/unit/app/window_pointer_test.cpp:184` and `:119`), 8 of 10 `[wheel]` cases still
   green — the pass-through and non-consume assertions are *supposed* to survive this mutation,
   and that they did is what says the red is landing on the reset and not on the field.
