2. **`wheel.reset()` moved after `glfwPollEvents()`** in `app/main.cpp`. Predicted: the order
   pin `REQUIRE(reset < poll)` fails. **It did**, at `viewport_test.cpp`, `11497 < 11479`. This
   is the half of the slice no headless test can execute, and the pin is written as an **order**
   rather than as two substring checks for precisely this mutation: both lines being present is
   exactly what the broken order also satisfies.
3. **The tick gate removed** (`drop_observed` clears unconditionally) — the mutation for the
   zero-tick fix. Predicted: *"a frame that runs no ticks keeps its wheel delta"* at expected
   `3.0`, actual `0.0`, and *"an unconsumed wheel delta saturates…"* at expected `120.0`, actual
   `0.0`, with the frame-loop and sums cases **still green**. **All three predictions exact**
   (`window_pointer_test.cpp:231` and `:254`; 11 of 13 `[wheel]` cases green).
4. **The saturation clamp removed** (plain `+=`). Predicted: only the saturation case, expected
   `120.0`, actual `10000.0`. **Exact** (`window_pointer_test.cpp:254`, 12 of 13 green).
5. **`glfwSetWindowUserPointer` re-added to `app/main.cpp`.** Run because the coordinator made
   the right point about the *other* textual pin: a pin caught by reading `normalize_ws` rather
   than by a red test has an unproven ability to fail at all. Predicted: only the new
   user-pointer ban. **Exact** (`viewport_test.cpp:607`, `11145 == npos` failing, 12 of 13
   green).
6. **`drop_observed` emptied — AND THE ONE I GOT WRONG.** I predicted two red: the frame-loop
   test's final assertion (`3.0` vs `0.0`) and the sums test (`2.5` vs `0.0`). Both failed with
   exactly those values — **and a third did too**, `window_pointer_test.cpp:234`, the
   *"and only now is it dropped"* tail of the zero-tick test, `3.0f == Approx( 0.0 )`. **The
   cause is a flaw in how I enumerated:** I predicted one failing assertion per test case, and
   the zero-tick test asserts *two different things* — that the gate preserves a delta nothing
   observed, and that the clear eventually fires. A mutation to the clear reddens the second
   while leaving the first green. Recorded because the under-prediction is the same shape as
   calling a test "the one that catches X": a test case is not a claim, an assertion is.

**The field is last, and the comment says why in terms gcc will not.** `{x, y, down}` and
`{0.0f, 0.0f, false, false}` both appear in the tree. A `float` inserted before `inside` would
re-bind them **silently**: `false` is an integral constant representable in `float`, so
`bool -> float` is not a narrowing conversion in list-initialisation and nothing is diagnosed.
Every aggregate initialisation of `PointerState` in the repo was enumerated: **124 of them**.
Three are production — `app/run/window_pointer.hpp`'s degenerate-window return and
`app/main.cpp`'s no-window return, both **widened explicitly** to `{…, 0.0f}` rather than left
to the default, so a reader sees the intent; and `ClickNudgeSystem`'s `PointerState{}` fallback
for an absent provider, where the defaulted `0.0f` is already the right answer. The other 121
are tests: **114 three-element** (102 of them `h.ptr = {x, y, down}` braced assignments in
`ui_system_test.cpp` alone, 10 in `ui_hit_test.cpp`, 2 in `render_system_test.cpp`), **4
four-element** `{x, y, down, inside}` in `ui_system_test.cpp`, and **3 empty** `{}`. The
default `0.0f` in last position leaves all 124 meaning exactly what they meant, which is why
the diff touches none of them.

**`window_pointer_state` gained a sixth parameter with NO default**, so a call site that forgets
the wheel is a compile error rather than a silently zero delta that only a textual pin could
catch. Thirteen existing test calls were updated to pass `/*wheel_delta=*/0.0`.

**One trap worth repeating.** The first version of the `studio_app.cpp` pin was written with
the source's own `=` alignment (`sample.wheel           = …`) and could never have matched:
`read_repo_source` runs `normalize_ws`, which collapses whitespace runs to one space. The same
helper's comment already records the sibling lesson about `code_only` stripping comments; this
is its whitespace half.

**Counts.** Unit **1584 → 1597** (seven wheel cases on the standalone policy and accumulator,
two on the editor provider, two `app/main.cpp` textual pins, one `UiSystem` no-op regression,
one editor-wiring pin). Renderer gate re-run although this slice touches no render path: exit
**124**, three `[INFO]`, zero VUIDs, and **byte-identical** to the capture taken before the
zero-tick fix. `mosaic run --window` under `timeout`: exit 124, stderr carrying only the two
known IBL-bake `[INFO]` lines.

**What the handoff did NOT get, and why.** The window-user-pointer hazard is strong landmine
material — an hour lost, no gate able to see it — but `next-steps.md` had 77 bytes of headroom
under its 8 KB ceiling and the entry needs ~250. The judgement: the textual pin closes the only
live route permanently and fails loudly with the reason attached, which is a stronger guard than
a paragraph a reader may not reach. Flagged to the coordinator rather than decided silently.

## U9d — `UiScroll`, and the renderer gate turns out never to reach a UI draw (2026-10-27)

**Specs:** [decisions](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-27-u9d-ui-scroll-decisions.md) (the
supervisor's rulings, written before any code),
[plan](../superpowers/plans/2026-10-27-u9d-ui-scroll.md) (the oracle, O1-O4, written before
any measurement). Completes [U9](../superpowers/specs/2026-10-26-u9-clipping-scroll-decomposition.md).

`UiScroll{offset, content}`, both in container-height multiples, authored beside a
`UiClipRect`. The whole feature is one subtraction in `resolve_parent_frame`:
`frame.y -= scroll_shift_for_children(...)`. That hook was chosen because its own header
comment already explains why it is the only place that reaches all four consumers — panels,
images and button hit-tests arrive via `resolve_ui_rect`, but the HUD **text** pass calls
`resolve_parent_frame` directly, so hooking the wrapper would have moved every panel and
silently left every label behind.

### The finding: the renderer gate exits before the UI passes ever run

This is the part worth reading, and it devalues evidence claimed across the whole U9/U11 arc,
including claims I made as the supervisor.

The project's renderer gate is
`timeout 12 env MOSAIC_VK_SYNC_VALIDATION=1 ./build/debug/app/mosaic editor`, passing on exit
**124** with three `[INFO]` lines and zero VUIDs. U9a and U9b each reported it
**byte-identical** before and after, and the honest reading offered at the time was "vacuous
for clipping, because no scene authors a `UiClipRect`". U9d authored the first one — and the
gate was *still* byte-identical.

So the gate was made to fail on purpose, which is the only way to learn what a green gate
means. `clip_to_pixel_rect` was mutated to emit a scissor offset 100000 pixels outside the
render area: **gate green.** Then to emit a *negative* offset, which Vulkan genuinely forbids
(`offset.x` must be >= 0): **gate still green.** A probe that cannot fail is worse than no
probe, so the next question was whether the code ran at all. A `fprintf` in the HUD panel
pass answered it:

| `timeout` | UI panel passes executed | clipped draws |
|---|---|---|
| 12 s (**the gate as documented**) | **0** | **0** |
| 15 s | **0** | **0** |
| 18 s | 4872 | 1176 |
| 25 s | 11704 | 3724 |

**At the gate's own 12-second budget, zero UI draws happen.** The first HUD frame lands
between 15 and 18 seconds on this machine. Not a sync-validation artefact: `timeout 12` with
sync validation off, and `timeout 12 ./mosaic run --window`, both also reach zero. The
startup — asset cook, pipeline creation and the IBL bake, whose "ready" line *does* print
inside 12 s — consumes the whole window before the first UI frame.

**What the gate does prove,** and it is not nothing: instance/device/swapchain creation, the
asset and IBL path, a clean object graph through that window, and zero sync-validation
messages during it. **What it does not prove, and never has:** anything whatsoever about any
UI draw — clipped or not, scrolled or not. Every "renderer gate identical" line in the U9 and
U11 entries above should be read with that scope.

It is the same defect as the one already recorded for validation counts — asserting zero
messages is vacuous unless you also assert the thing being watched actually ran — except here
the unasserted precondition is "a frame got drawn". A green gate currently cannot be told
apart from a gate that timed out during startup, and **that** is the fix, rather than simply
raising the timeout: the gate needs to *assert it reached a UI frame*. Backlogged with both
halves, because changing the project's primary renderer gate is its own slice with its own
evidence and folding it into U9d would make U9d unreviewable.

**Clipping is not unverified**, to be clear about what survives: `tests/gpu/` covers it on a
real `VulkanRenderDevice` offscreen with pixel assertions, and those 12 tests pass. The
coverage was always there — it was the *gate* that was being over-credited.

### The oracle earned its keep twice

**O1 (a scroll translates the children and nothing else moves)** is asserted bit-exactly on
`x`, `w` and `h` rather than with a tolerance, because the decomposition's stated reason for
forbidding clipping from reusing this hook is that `resolve_parent_frame` "relocates **and**
resizes". Mutation M2 added `frame.h -= shift` beside the `frame.y` subtraction — exactly that
failure — and landed on `ui_scroll_test.cpp:96`, `REQUIRE(at_half.h == at0.h)`, `0.025f ==
0.05f`. An `Approx` would have swallowed it at small offsets.

**And O1's second case was written because the first cannot see a scale.** One child moving by
the right amount is consistent with a subtree being scaled; "every child moves by the *same*
delta" is not. Mutation M1 (delete the shift) proved why the pair is needed: its **first**
assertion, `(a1.y-a0.y) == Approx(b1.y-b0.y)`, **passes on the broken code** — 0 == 0 — and
only the second, against the expected `-0.25`, goes red at `:122`. The "container, not slot"
case passes on M1 entirely, for the same reason. Predicted in writing before the run, and that
is the habit worth keeping: *a test case is not a claim; an assertion is.*

### Mutations, predicted beside achieved

| | Mutation | Predicted | Achieved | Gap |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| M1 | delete the shift | O1-translate `:98`, O1-same-delta's **second** assertion, O4; and O1-"container not slot" **green on broken code** | exactly that — `:98` `0.2f == Approx(0.1)`, `:122` `0.0f == Approx(-0.25)`, `:336` `!true`; 3 of 1621 red | none |
| M2 | also shrink the frame (`frame.h -=`) | O1-translate's `h` assertion only | `:96` `0.025f == 0.05f`; 2 red | none |
| M3 | drop the clip filter in `button_hit_candidates` | O4, **and U9b's authored-clip cases too** (not exclusive) | O4 plus `#1293`, `#1296`, `#1297`; 4 red | none |

### Two traps hit while doing it

**A `ctest` run against a binary the build had not finished producing.** M1 first reported
**1621/1621 green** — i.e. "my tests do not test the scroll at all". The mutation *was*
applied (`grep -c` confirmed the line gone). Comparing mtimes showed the test binary was
**older than the edited header**: the build had not completed before `ctest` ran. Rebuilt, and
the three predicted failures appeared. This is the stale-binary landmine in a new form — the
existing one is about `mv` preserving mtime after a *restore* — and it was one step away from
being recorded as a false finding about my own tests. **Check the binary is newer than the
source before believing a mutation result**, in either direction.

**An 8-second windowed run that never left the IBL bake.** The first clip probe reported zero
calls in both runtimes; the run's entire output was the single line
`[INFO] demo: baking environment IBL`, without the matching `ready`. Nothing was wrong with
the probe — it had not been reached. It is the same shape as the gate finding above, and it is
why the table has a column for "did the code run" rather than only "was it green".

### The authored region, and the eye pass it exists for

Mid-right, anchored `Right`, deliberately clear of the top-left HUD, the top-right stack and
the bottom-left layout-only container, so a human looking at it cannot be confused about which
feature they are seeing. `content = 2.5` container-heights against a region 0.30 tall, six
colour rows, a `UiButton` at the top that scrolls out, and one label carrying **both** a `\n`
and a `wrap_width` — because U11b shipped word-wrap that nothing authored, so no human had seen
it. **No Transform and no script on any entity in it**, which keeps the four default-scene
count tests out of the slice entirely.

`kScrollNotch = 0.1f` container-heights is **derived, not tuned**: `layout_text` maps a label's
`height` to `height * frame.h` per line, and the region's label is authored at `height = 0.1`,
so one notch is exactly one line of that label. Checkable arithmetic rather than a feel, which
matters because nobody has scrolled it.

**Adding the region broke a test, and the test was right.**
`demo_scene_test.cpp:1015` asserts `compared == 6`: a *denominator* guard over top-level HUD
panels and images, there so that "assert over whatever we find" cannot go silently vacuous. A
seventh top-level panel is exactly what it is designed to catch, and its other 16 assertions —
the ones checking the flow block does not overlap anything — all passed, which is what
confirmed the placement. Updated to 7 and named the new element, rather than relaxing it.

### Three fixture fixes the verification pass itself forced

Writing the human checklist found three things wrong with the authored region that no test
could see, because they are all "can a person actually check this?" problems. The page is the
artifact that found them, which is the argument for writing it before merging rather than after.

**The button had no visible response at all.** `scroll_demo_click` is emitted as a script event
nothing subscribes to, so a click produced no effect, and the button carried no `UiHoverStyle` —
meaning the central item ("scroll it out of the clip, then check it stops responding") was
**unobservable as authored**. Added a `UiHoverStyle{tint 1.5}`, which is the only thing that
makes "is this button still live?" answerable by eye. The HUD bar already had one; the new
button did not, and nothing would have complained.

**The wrapped label overlapped the button.** Resolved arithmetic: the region is
`{0.71, 0.35, 0.26, 0.30}`, a label at `height = 0.1` gives a line step of `0.1 * 0.30 = 0.03`
screen Y, and the original 58-character string wrapped to roughly four lines from `y = 0.362`,
reaching `~0.47` — exactly where the button sat. Two overlapping features in one small box makes
an eye check ambiguous about which one it is looking at. The string is now short enough to wrap
to about three lines, and the button and rows moved down (`pos.y` 0.26 -> 0.40, rows
`0.40 + 0.34i` -> `0.55 + 0.34i`).

**The region was unfindable in the editor.** The Hierarchy labels a row with the entity's `Name`
or falls back to `Entity <index>`, and **no demo entity was named**, so the `field_bool` item
would have required hunting for an index. The region now carries `Name{"Scroll region"}` — the
only named entity in the demo. `Name` is neither a Transform nor a script, so the four
count tests are unaffected (confirmed: 1621 still green).

The resolved geometry those fixes were checked against, dumped from `build_default_scene`
itself rather than computed by hand: region `0.71-0.97` x `0.35-0.65`; at offset 0 the button
(`y 0.470-0.500`) and row 0 are fully visible and **row 1 is cut by the bottom edge**, with rows
2-5 entirely clipped; max offset `1.5` container-heights = `0.45` screen Y = **exactly 15
notches**; at full scroll rows 3-5 are visible and the button is fully clipped. Those are the
numbers the checklist's success conditions are anchored on, which is the difference between a
condition and a hope.

**And one measurement the pass needed that nothing else had asked for:** the first tick fires at
**16.90 s** (`[INFO] run: tick 60 (16.90s)`). Scrolling runs in `UiSystem::update`, so **the
wheel does nothing for the first ~17 seconds** of a windowed run. Without that on the page a
reader would have reported "the wheel does not work" against a working build. It is the same
startup cost as the gate finding above, seen from the user's side.

### What no gate here can see, stated rather than implied

Nothing in this slice observes a pixel or how the scroll *looks*. Ruling 1 shipped the offset
**unsnapped**: no pixel snapping exists anywhere in the UI path (verified — no rounding in
`engine/ui/`, and `ui.vert` maps a normalized float rect straight to clip space), so glyph
quads land on sub-pixel positions and the font is a **bitmap** atlas, not SDF. Soft or
shimmering text while scrolling is the predicted symptom and the eye pass is the only thing
that can judge it; if it shimmers, that observation is the trigger for the backlogged snapping
option. The weaker sentence is the true one: **no gate observed a positioning artefact**, which
is not the same as "it looks right".

## The U9 verification pass: six items, all passed (2026-10-28)

**The [checklist](https://claude.ai/artifact/3SfMHv7xQXaYKWfT2V8yiE) gated PR #327 and the
results were read back from its datastore, not from a summary.** Six items, answered between
12:05 and 12:10 — a real five-minute pass rather than a blanket tick. Verdicts, by item id:
`clip-visible` pass, `dead-button` pass, `shimmer` pass, `scroll-clamp` pass, `wrapped-text`
pass, `field-bool` pass. No notes were left on any item.

**What that bought, in claims this project could not otherwise make:**

- **Clipping has been seen working.** U9a and U9b shipped the clip rect and the hierarchy walk
  with no authored clip anywhere, and the renderer gate could not have seen one anyway (above).
  A human has now confirmed content is cut at the container boundary and that only the first two
  rows reach the screen.
- **A scrolled-out button is dead.** The U8b bug class — a clip that hides pixels and leaves the
  button live — is confirmed absent on a real scroll, by hover brightness, which is the only
  visible evidence a click produces (the authored event has no subscriber).
- **Wrapped text has been read by a person.** U11b shipped `\n` and word-wrap that nothing
  authored; "SCROLL ME" on its own line and the sentence wrapping at spaces is now observed
  rather than asserted.
- **`field_bool` works, and a clip survives an invisible container.** One click closed an item
  owed since the U8 arc and confirmed `UiClipRect`'s documented property 1 — a container clips
  its children even when its own panel is `visible = false` — which no gate reaches.

**The decision item, and its owner.** `shimmer` was a decision, not a verdict: pass meant
*keep the scroll offset unsnapped*. It was taken that way. Recorded beside the code it governs
(`ui_hierarchy.hpp`, at the subtraction) and in the backlog entry, which is now **declined**
rather than deferred, so the next reader does not implement snapping from the argument alone.
**Stated honestly: no reasoning was recorded with it.** The verdict is therefore "a human
looked at this display, at this scale, and it was fine", which is weaker than "sub-pixel
bitmap scrolling is fine in general" and must not be tidied into the stronger sentence.

**No verdict needs re-checking.** The skill's rule is that a pass which produces a fix can
invalidate another item's verdict, because the verdicts were given against the build the
reader ran and not the one that ships. Here all six passed, nothing was fixed afterwards, and
the build they were judged on is the one that merged — so every verdict rests on the shipped
appearance. The three fixture fixes recorded above all landed *before* the pass, not after it.

**Two claims the fixture could not reach** were named on the page rather than quietly dropped,
and remain unverified: a `WorldLabel` containing `\n` (nothing authors one, and its vertical
centring is known wrong), and whether the `'?'` fallback glyph reads as an error rather than
as content (no authored label is missing a glyph). Both are the next pass's material if the
fixture grows.

## `docs/updates/` was reopened by accident, and the notice now defends itself (2026-10-28)

While local `main` was being fast-forwarded after #327, `git reset --keep` refused — correctly
— because the working tree held five uncommitted changes made from outside this session: a new
dated summary in `docs/updates/`, which `CLAUDE.md` closes, and an edit to
`docs/updates/README.md` **deleting the "This directory is closed. Do not add files here."
line**, so the violation also removed the statement that made it one. Present alongside it: a
competing `**Last updated:**` narrative block prepended to the top of this file (the convention
here is dated sections appended at the end), a 410-line rewrite of
`docs/project/advancement-plans-and-evaluation.md`, and a regenerated `docs/index.html`. The
summary also already carried a stale figure — "1608 unit and integration tests", true at #326
and 1621 by then.

**Discarded at the human's instruction**, and backed up first, because uncommitted work from
another session is not this one's to destroy on its own judgement. No code was involved and
`origin/main` was never affected. The human judged it a one-off with a cause already known to
them and **declined a CI guard** — the proportionate call for a docs hiccup, and the reason no
`docs/updates/` check exists.

**What changed as a result is only the notation.** `docs/updates/README.md` now states where
the rule actually lives (`CLAUDE.md`), that deleting the notice does not reopen the directory,
and that a dated narrative of recent work is an appended `docs/record/` section. The weakness
was never that the rule was unclear — `CLAUDE.md` says "`docs/updates/` is closed; do not add
files there" in as many words. It was that the rule's only *visible* statement sat inside the
very file a writer heading for that directory would open and edit, which made the mistake
self-concealing rather than merely wrong.

## The landmine list gets its own document: three documents become four (2026-10-28)

**The handoff breached its 8 KB ceiling for the third time, and the third time was
structural rather than careless.** `next-steps.md` sat at 8170 of 8192 bytes with the easy
compressions already spent, and the byte breakdown says why:

| section | bytes |
|---|---|
| preamble | 257 |
| `## State` | 776 |
| `## Next work` | 1260 |
| **`## Landmines`** | **5585** |
| `## Pointers` | 292 |

**68% of a document whose entire job is to be short.** The two largest entries were both
added that same day — the renderer gate covering startup only (970 bytes) and the GLFW
window-user-pointer collision (581) — and both earned their place, which is the problem. A
ceiling applied to a list that grows one entry per scar means the scar list gets pruned for
space rather than because a scar healed. `CLAUDE.md`'s own "why the split exists" paragraph
already said this: *any document that mixes the thing that grows forever with something else
inherits unbounded growth.* It named only the record as that thing. The landmine list is the
second one, and nobody had noticed.

So: **`docs/project/landmines.md`**, and `CLAUDE.md` now documents **four documents, four
jobs**. The handoff went **8170 -> 2953 bytes** and is pointers again.

**I recommended the wrong tool first, twice, and that is worth recording.** Told the human the
fix was `tools/docs/archive_next_steps.py` — "the tool for it" — on the strength of its name
and the one line about it in `CLAUDE.md`, without opening it. Its `--dry-run` answers
`no dated blocks found; nothing to do`, and it could never have helped: it moves dated
`**Last updated:**` narrative blocks, which `CLAUDE.md` forbids this document from ever
containing again. The tool was retired for this file by the 2026-10-21 split and nothing said
so. Same denominator error as the four already recorded this month — a confident claim drawn
from a narrower reading than the claim needed.

**The move was conservation-checked, with both controls, because moving text between docs is
exactly the operation that quietly destroys history.** The check asserts every one of the 16
bullets is present after the split, byte-identical and **exactly once**, across the two files
together. Positive control: the real move passes, 16/16. Negative controls, both of which must
fail and did: dropping the last bullet was caught and named (`Screen-space (non-MVP)
shaders...`), and mangling a single byte inside one (`exit **124**` -> `125`) was caught and
named (`ctest` sees nothing of the renderer...). A conservation check without a negative
control licenses trust it has not earned — `archive_next_steps.py`'s own docstring says so,
and the discipline was copied from it deliberately.

**References updated rather than left dangling:** the State table's "see landmines" now links
the new file, `## Pointers` gained a row for it, and `CLAUDE.md`'s handoff bullet no longer
lists landmines among the handoff's contents. The 2026-10-21 architecture design spec is left
as written — it records the decision as it was made, and amendments belong here and in
`CLAUDE.md`, not in a rewritten spec.

**What this does not fix.** The landmine list still has no retirement pressure: 16 entries
today, and the only thing stopping it reaching 60 is somebody judging that a trap is gone. The
new document says to retire only on that basis and never for space, which converts a silent
size problem into an explicit judgement — but a judgement nobody is scheduled to make. If it
grows past being readable, that is the next finding, and it will not announce itself.

## The handoff gets its next slices back, with gates (2026-10-29)

Asked whether the docs were handoff-ready, the answer was "almost" — and the gap was the
handoff failing **its own documented job**. `CLAUDE.md` says it carries *"current state with the
command that verifies each claim, and the next two or three slices with their gates."* It had
the state. It had no slices and no gates: it listed what had finished and pointed at two tier
rows by number, so a cold agent would have known what was done and nothing about what to start.
It also opened with "No time-sensitive item is open" while the month's highest-value finding —
the renderer gate proving startup only — sat unnamed in the backlog.

**Four slices now, in order, each with the observation that ends it**, at the human's
instruction (the recommendation was the same four; the choice of how many was theirs):

1. the renderer gate must assert it reached a UI frame — gate: forcing a bad scissor turns it
   **red**, where both an out-of-area and a negative offset leave it green today, which is the
   control that has to flip;
2. `tests/gpu/`'s zero-validation assertion must stop being vacuous — gate: disabling the layer
   makes the tests **fail rather than pass**;
3. the `begin_frame` counter resets need a behavioural test — gate: deleting one reset turns a
   test red, and the chunk-count inspection seam it needs *is* the slice;
4. a staleness check on the `Checked` column — gate: changing a `Value` without its date fails,
   changing both passes.

**Two of the four are the same defect class as the finding that produced them:** an assertion
that cannot fail. Ordering them first is deliberate — until (1) lands, every future "renderer
gate identical" line means startup is unchanged and nothing more, which is exactly how four
such claims got made this month.

**Two smaller corrections in the same pass.** The suite rows read `Checked 2026-10-27` while
their values were re-verified on the 29th, which is precisely what slice 4 exists to catch;
dates moved to the 29th. And this file's own header described itself as fed by
`archive_next_steps.py` — true as provenance for the dated blocks below, misleading as a live
mechanism, since the tool is retired for that path and `CLAUDE.md` forbids the handoff from ever
carrying a dated block again. The header now says new entries are appended directly.

Handoff: **2953 -> 5177 bytes**, of 8192. The ceiling is no longer the binding constraint; the
landmine split bought the room for the document to do its job properly.

## A staleness check on `next-steps.md`'s `Checked` column, and the stricter rule that lost to its own history

Slice 4 of the four the #330 handoff named. `tools/docs/check_checked_column_staleness.py`
compares the State table across a base and a new revision and fails when a row's `Value`
changed but its `Checked` cell is **older than the commit that changed it**. Wired into
`ci.yml` on `pull_request` only (it needs a base revision), beside the claims checker, with
`actions/checkout` moved to `fetch-depth: 50` because the attribution needs the PR's commits.

**The premise was right about the defect and wrong about its date.** The handoff said the two
suite rows "read 2026-10-27 while the values were re-verified on the 29th". The git record is
that #327 authored `1621 tests` / `1621/1621` on the **28th** and left both rows at
`Checked 2026-10-27`; the 29th is when #330 noticed and moved them. Either way the cell was
older than the number beside it, which is what the check now asserts.

**The rule the gate asked for would have been a seven-false-positive-for-one-real-defect
trade, and running it over real history is what showed that.** The handoff's gate reads "editing
a `Value` and leaving its `Checked` date alone fails the check". Implemented literally — the cell
must *differ* — it reds **8 of the 20 most recent commits** to touch the handoff, and in **7** of
them the date already equalled the commit's own date, because the suite was re-run and the number
updated on the day the cell already named. Nothing needed to change; the rule demanded an edit
that would have made the document *less* true. The coordinator's ruling made the property
freshness relative to the change instead, which keeps the one real catch (#327, and #313 before
it, both a day stale) and drops all seven. The same scan is the regression evidence: 2 of 20 red,
both pre-existing on `main` and both already self-healed by later commits, so no base ≥ #330 is
red today. The ruling and the dead rule are written into the module docstring, not only here,
because "tighten it to require the cell to differ" is the obvious next edit.

**Attribution is per row and per commit** (the `git log -S`-per-cell part that got the slice
deferred in the first place): the checker walks every commit in `base..new` that touched the
document, parses the table at each, and dates each `Claim`'s last `Value` change to that commit's
**author** date via `--date=short`, i.e. the author's own calendar, not the runner's and not UTC
— the cell is typed by a human reading their own calendar. Per row matters: an unrelated later
commit to the document cannot re-date an honest row. Judging the final state also makes the
multi-commit case pass by construction, proven on real history — `130ef47..1ae0174`, where the
value moved on the 28th and the date on the 29th, is green, while `f79c076` alone is red.

**Both controls, because a new checker's likeliest failure is flagging everything.** 29 unit
tests over self-contained fixtures (throwaway git repos in a temp dir, never this repo's history):
the negative control is a `Value` changed with the date left behind; the positive controls are
same-day re-verification, value and date both moved, a date already ahead, nothing changed, a row
added wholesale, a row removed, a renamed `Claim`, a reworded `Verify with`, and a whitespace-only
reflow. On the live document: editing the `Build` row's `Value` alone is red naming both dates,
the same edit with the date moved to today is green, and rewording its `Verify with` alone is green.

## Slice 1 — the renderer gate learns to assert it reached a UI frame (2026-10-30)

**The premise held, and was worse than written.** Re-measured on this branch before any code
was written: `timeout 12 env MOSAIC_VK_SYNC_VALIDATION=1 ./build/debug/app/mosaic editor` exits
124 with three `[INFO]` lines and zero VUID/ERROR/WARN **with a deliberately invalid scissor
forced into `clip_to_pixel_rect`** — both faults the handoff named (`out.x = x0 - 4`, a negative
offset; `out.width = (x1 - x0) + 8`, an extent running past the render area). A 25 s control run
was green too, which at the time read as "the gate is blind"; the instrumentation added later in
the slice showed the truer and worse answer — **that run had not reached a clipped UI draw yet**,
so whether the gate noticed depended on wall-clock luck. A gate whose pass is indistinguishable
from a startup timeout asserts nothing, and four "renderer gate identical" claims this month rest
on it.

**Two premise corrections.** The handoff said 0 UI panel passes at 15 s and 4872 at 18 s. On this
branch the first UI draw lands somewhere **between 12 s and 25 s** — 12 s reaches none, 25 s
reaches one, reproducibly across five runs. The 18 s figure is not reproducible here and the
wall-clock threshold should not be treated as a constant; it is exactly the kind of number a
gate must not depend on. Second: **there is no gate script.** The gate existed only as a
sentence in `landmines.md`, so neither `ctest` nor CI could ever run it. That is why it never
failed — nothing was executing it.

**What the slice added.** `modules/render_vulkan/vulkan_ui_draw_stats.hpp/.cpp`: a `UiDrawStats`
counter triple (`ui_draws`, `clipped_ui_draws`, `out_of_area_scissors`) plus
`scissor_in_render_area`, four integer comparisons implementing the three VUIDs
`vkCmdSetScissor` enforces. `VulkanRenderDevice` increments `ui_draws` inside all three
`draw_ui*` paths **after their early returns**, so it counts recorded draws and not intentions,
and `set_ui_clip` pushes every scissor through `record_ui_clip_scissor`. The counters are
readable through a new `UiDrawStatsSource` interface, reached in tests via a third field on
`OffscreenDevice` — the same non-owning shape `readback` already uses.

**Why the check is arithmetic and not the validation layer.** Delegating it would have inherited
slice 2's defect: the layer is compiled in only `#ifndef NDEBUG` and used only when installed.
That was not a theoretical worry — **measured, the layer caught only one of the two faults in a
windowed run.** Fault A (negative offset) produced `VUID-vkCmdSetScissor-x-00595` three times;
fault B (out-of-area extent) produced **no validation output at all**, in three consecutive 25 s
runs. The project's own arithmetic check caught both.

**The new gate.** `tests/gpu/offscreen_ui_frame_reached_test.cpp`, labelled `[gpu]`, carried by
`ctest -L gpu`, opt-in locally and already running on lavapipe in CI. It asserts a fresh device
has recorded nothing, drives one frame of six clips (interior, overhanging every side, running
off the far corner, entirely off-surface, negative extent, and flush at the far corner) plus one
unclipped draw, then asserts **`ui_draws == 7`** before asserting `out_of_area_scissors == 0`.
The reached-a-frame assertion is the precondition the old gate assumed and never checked.
Eight new unit cases in `tests/unit/render/ui_draw_stats_test.cpp` pin the predicate itself,
including the int32/uint32 sum that wraps and the zero extent that is *legal*.

**Three fault injections, all three red for the predicted reason.**

| Fault | Assertion that fired | Message |
|---|---|---|
| `out.x = x0 - 4` | `REQUIRE(after.out_of_area_scissors == 0)` | `1 == 0`, `offset=(-4, 0) extent=(96 x 64) against a render area of 96 x 64`, with `ui_draws=7` |
| `out.width = (x1-x0) + 8u` | same | `4 == 0`, `offset=(0, 0) extent=(104 x 64)` |
| `draw_ui` returns early | `REQUIRE(after.ui_draws == clips.size() + 1u)` | `0 == 7`, `ui_draws=0 clipped_ui_draws=0` |

The third is the one that matters most: it is the defect class the old gate was blind to, and it
fails on the counter rather than on a pixel, so the message says *why*.

**The GUI gate is improved too, not retired.** An out-of-area scissor now logs once at ERROR
(`ui clip scissor OUT OF AREA: ...`), which makes the gate's existing "zero VUID/ERROR/WARN"
criterion able to catch a bad scissor without depending on the layer. Measured: fault B now
makes the GUI gate RED in three of three 25 s runs where it was green before. Two one-shot
`[INFO]` lines (`first UI draw recorded`, `first clipped UI draw recorded`) make "did it reach a
UI frame" greppable instead of guessed, which is what allowed the 12 s/25 s bisection above.
**The pass condition therefore changed: six `[INFO]` lines at 25 s, not three at 12 s**, and the
two UI lines must be PRESENT — their absence is now a failure rather than the normal case.

**An unexpected finding, recorded because nothing else would.** The ERROR line printed
`render area=1 x 1`. In the editor, `ui_destination_extent()` returns `vp_extent_`, and while the
viewport pass is open that is **1 x 1** — so every clipped UI draw the GUI gate exercises is
computed against a degenerate one-pixel destination. It also explains fault B's silence: an 8 x 1
scissor is illegal against what the *code believes* the destination is and legal against the real
render pass area, so the layer had nothing to say. This is not fixed here; it is 🟡 row 10's
viewport-readback seam, and it is now in the backlog with these numbers attached.

**The landmine was corrected, not retired.** `ctest` still sees no `VkDevice` and the GUI gate
is still not UI coverage on its own, so the trap survives in a changed shape; the entry now
names the GPU gate as the thing that does assert a UI frame, and gives the new pass condition.

**Predicted beside achieved.** Suite 1621 -> 1629 (+8 unit, predicted +8); GPU 12 -> 13
(predicted +1); unit suite 6.0 s, GPU 2.5 s. One gap: the slice was scoped as a replacement for
the GUI gate and ended up keeping both, because the GUI gate turned out to be cheap to make
non-vacuous once the counters existed — the ERROR line is nine lines of code.

## Making the GPU tests' zero-validation assertion non-vacuous (2026-10-30)

The handoff's slice 2 said eight assertion sites across seven GPU tests "pass for free wherever
the layer is absent". **That premise was largely wrong, and finding out how it was wrong is the
result of this slice.**

**The control, run on unmodified `main` at 1ae0174.** `VK_LOADER_LAYERS_DISABLE='*' ctest
--preset debug -L gpu` did **not** pass: **10 of 12 failed**. The seven offscreen tests failed
at `REQUIRE(validation_messages.empty())` — but for the wrong reason and by accident. When the
loader stops enumerating the layer, `create_instance` logs `[WARN] vulkan: validation layer
'VK_LAYER_KHRONOS_validation' not available; continuing without it`, and that WARN is itself a
`vulkan`-category warning, which the capture sink (installed *before* device creation in every
one of the seven) duly captured. So `empty()` was false because the backend had complained about
the layer, and the diagnosis a reader got was `REQUIRE( validation_messages.empty() )` with
expansion `false` — which reads as a Vulkan validation error in the renderer. Wrong cause, wrong
place to look.

**Nothing pinned that crutch.** Re-word that log line, change its category or its level, or move
a sink installation to after device creation, and all seven go quietly vacuous with no test edit
and no review signal. The two cases that *were* already silent prove the shape: the two in
`offscreen_ui_clip_hierarchy_test.cpp` passed under the disabled layer, and one of them carries
the comment "and, just as importantly, does so without a validation error" while capturing no
messages at all — a written claim the test did not make.

**Where the premise WAS right: `#ifndef NDEBUG`.** Preprocessing the real compile command for
`vulkan_render_device.cpp` with the flag flipped: `-UNDEBUG` yields 1 occurrence of
`VK_LAYER_KHRONOS_validation`, `-DNDEBUG` yields **0**. In a release GPU build the layer, the
messenger and the absence-warning all cease to exist, so the crutch is gone and every site is
vacuous. The guard was not touched — the fix is in the tests, as the slice required.

**What shipped.** One free function on the render-device header,
`validation_messages_are_observed()`, returning a `bool` and leaking no Vulkan or GLFW type into
a header that deliberately forward-declares `GLFWwindow`. It is **process-global, not
per-device**, and deliberately read *after* the device is destroyed — the question is "did
validation run for the device this test just exercised", which destruction does not un-answer. A
stale `true` is prevented by resetting the flag at the top of every `create_instance`, outside
the `NDEBUG` guard so that a build which cannot validate reports `false` rather than nothing. It
is published at the end of `setup_debug_messenger`, and the value published is the *conjunction*
of layer-enabled and messenger-created: the messenger alone would be a lie, because the
debug-utils extension is pushed unconditionally and the messenger is therefore created even when
the layer is absent, where it simply never has anything to report.

A free function rather than the existing `VulkanHeadlessDevice::validation_enabled()` accessor
because the seven tests do not use `VulkanHeadlessDevice` at all — they use
`create_offscreen_vulkan_render_device`, i.e. the real `VulkanRenderDevice`, whose class is
file-local to the .cpp and has no public surface to hang an accessor on. The handoff's pointer to
`vulkan_headless_device.hpp:30` was therefore a pointer at the wrong class; that accessor is used
only by `headless_device_test.cpp` and `offscreen_readback_test.cpp`, which have required it
since 2026-10-24. Two mechanisms for one question is now backlogged.

Test side: `tests/gpu/validation_capture.hpp`, holding the single `ValidationCaptureSink` (six
copies deleted) and `require_validation_observed_and_silent()`, which asserts **observed, then
silent** in that order so the failure names the cause. Its failure message names all three ways
the layer can be off, including NDEBUG. Ten call sites: the eight the slice named, plus the two
in `offscreen_ui_clip_hierarchy_test.cpp` that asserted nothing — a deliberate two-site extension,
because leaving them would have left the one defect the slice exists to remove.

**Measured.** Layer present: 12/12 green, and the default suite unchanged at 1621/1621.
Layer disabled: **12/12 red**, up from 10/12, every offscreen case firing
`REQUIRE( mosaic::render::vk::validation_messages_are_observed() )` at
`validation_capture.hpp:100` *first*, before the message count. Fault injection in the other
direction — deleting the publish in `setup_debug_messenger` with the layer fully loaded — takes
10/12 red at the same assertion, which proves it is reached and evaluated; restored (with
`touch`) it is 12/12 again.
The two survivors of that mutant are the two `VulkanHeadlessDevice` tests, which read their own
accessor and should not have moved.

**A prediction of this slice that the review falsified.** The sentence above originally read
that the deleted-publish mutant proved the flag "sourced from the real enablement path rather
than constant-true". It proved no such thing: deleting the publish makes the flag *false*, which
only shows the assertion is reached. **A wrong published value is a different fault and it is
invisible.** The reviewer injected it on 2026-10-30 against 401d027 — `store(true, …)` in place
of `store(validation_layer_enabled_, …)`, the exact lie the header comment claims to avoid — and
got **12/12 PASSING with the layer present**; with the layer off, all ten offscreen cases fell
through to `REQUIRE(validation_messages.empty())`, i.e. the new assertion passed vacuously and
the diagnosis regressed to the misleading message this slice existed to remove. Adding a second
one-line fault, recategorising the "validation layer not available" WARN off the `"vulkan"`
category so the sink stops collecting it, gave **83% passed, 2 of 12 failed** with the layer off:
ten GPU tests reporting "validation observed and silent" having observed nothing. The two that
caught it both times are the `VulkanHeadlessDevice` cases, which read per-device state instead of
the global — the control that already works.

So this slice closes the **environmental** route to vacuity (layer disabled, layer absent,
NDEBUG) and leaves the **mechanism's own correctness** unguarded, with the same accidental
log-category crutch standing between a one-line wrong store and ten silently vacuous tests. That
is the defect class one level up, it is now a 🟡 row rather than a tidiness bullet, and the header
comment above `validation_messages_are_observed()` says plainly that the conjunction is a
convention and not an enforced property — because a comment defended on a false premise is the
thing that makes the next reader trust what nothing checks.

**Decision: require, not skip.** The layer is asserted in every build rather than skipped where
absent, matching `headless_device_test.cpp` since 2026-10-24 and CI's own comment that
`vulkan-validationlayers` "is NOT optional". The consequence — a release-mode GPU build now fails
loudly instead of passing vacuously — is recorded in the backlog as a decision to revisit, not an
oversight.

## The `begin_frame` per-call counter resets get a behavioural test (2026-10-30)

Slice 3 of the four in the handoff. The three per-call counters (`particle_calls_`,
`ribbon_calls_`, `world_text_calls_`) are reset for the current frame-in-flight slot at the top
of `begin_frame`, and the only thing asserting that were three literal string searches in
`tests/unit/render/texture_colorspace_contract_test.cpp` — assertions that pass on a renamed
counter and fail on a reformat, and that cannot see behaviour at all.

**The handoff's line number was stale, and that was the first finding.** The resets were quoted
as `vulkan_render_device.cpp:1931-1933`; they are at `:1980-1982` on `1ae0174`. Every other
number in that entry checked out: the growth sites at `:1563-1564` (particles), `:1613-1614`
(ribbons) and `:1662-1663` (world text), the member declarations at `:6250`/`:6252`/`:6255`, and
the source-text coverage at `texture_colorspace_contract_test.cpp:383-385`. The stale one was
the one the entry led with — a reminder that a `file:line` in a planning document decays at the
rate the file is edited, which is why the backlog bullet it came from had carried the same wrong
number since 2026-10-24.

**The seam.** `vulkan_render_device.hpp` is 54 lines and forward-declares `GLFWwindow` rather
than including it, so the inspector had to carry no Vulkan and no GLFW type. It follows the
`OffscreenReadback` precedent exactly: an abstract class in the header, implemented by
`VulkanRenderDevice` (which now inherits all three), handed out as a non-owning pointer on a new
`OffscreenDevice::chunks` field. `StagingChunkCounts` is four `std::size_t`s — the three chunk
totals plus `frames_in_flight`, so a test can compute the expected total without knowing
`kFramesInFlight`.

**What is observed is the vector SIZE, not the counter.** The counters are reset every frame
either way; the residue a missed reset leaves is the chunk vector that grew and stays grown. So
the inspector reports `particle_instances_[i].size()` summed over the slots, not
`particle_calls_[i]`. Getting that backwards would have produced an inspector that reads 0 on
both the healthy and the broken device.

**The hazard the slice was warned about, confirmed empirically.** A missed reset grows buffers
without changing a pixel, so a pixel assertion would have been decorative. Each of the three
mutants was run against the whole GPU suite, and in all three runs **exactly one test failed** —
the new case for that counter. The three `offscreen_*_aliasing_test.cpp` pixel tests, which
exercise the very same code paths two calls per frame, stayed **green on every mutant**. That is
the direct measurement: 14 of 15 GPU tests on this branch, including every pixel test of these
three draw paths, cannot see a dropped reset. (15 of 16 on `main`, where slice 1's gate test
lands alongside; a reviewer reproduced the world-text mutant there — one failure out of 16.)

**The gate, three times.** Two calls per frame (not one: one call per frame cannot tell "at most
one chunk" from "at most one chunk per call"), one warm-up frame per in-flight slot, then six
more identical frames. With `kFramesInFlight == 2` the steady state is 4 chunks; the mutant
reaches 16.

| Deleted reset | Failing assertion | Expansion |
|---|---|---|
| `particle_calls_[frame_] = 0;` | `REQUIRE( after.particle == baseline.particle )` (`:143`) | `16 == 4` |
| `ribbon_calls_[frame_] = 0;` | `REQUIRE( after.ribbon == baseline.ribbon )` (`:209`) | `16 == 4` |
| `world_text_calls_[frame_] = 0;` | `REQUIRE( after.world_text == baseline.world_text )` (`:280`) | `16 == 4` |

Each mutant was restored with `cp` followed by `touch` — `mv` preserves mtime and ninja keeps the
mutant binary, which is a landmine here — then rebuilt and re-run: 15/15 green.

Note that the warm-up assertion (`baseline.particle == kCallsPerFrame * frames_in_flight`)
passes on all three mutants, because the first use of each slot allocates the same chunks either
way. It is kept as a precondition — it is what makes the no-growth comparison a comparison
against a known number rather than against whatever the device happened to do — but the
no-growth `REQUIRE` is the decisive one, and it is the one that fires.

**The source-text assertions were removed, not kept alongside.** The three string searches in
section 3 of "every per-frame staging buffer is chunked per call" are gone, with a comment in
their place naming the test that took them over. The reason, recorded here because deleting an
assertion needs an auditable one: they are wrong in both directions — vacuous after a rename,
red after a reformat — and a second assertion with those properties is a liability rather than a
belt and braces. **Sections 1, 2 and 4 of that case were kept:** they assert the chunked *form*
of the source, and `RenderSystem` makes exactly one call per path per frame, so no behavioural
test can reach an unchunked path through the engine. That is the part that genuinely has no
behavioural substitute, and the case's preamble now says which half is which.

**Verification.** GPU suite 15/15 (was 12) via `cmake -S . -B build/gpu -DMOSAIC_ENABLE_GPU_TESTS=ON`
then `ctest --test-dir build/gpu -L gpu`. **Those are the numbers measured on this slice's own
branch; it landed alongside slices 1 and 2, so on `main` the counts are 16 GPU and 1629 unit —
re-measured 16/16 green after the rebase.** Default suite 1621/1621 in 4.6 s here, unchanged in count —
the contract test case still exists, it just asserts three things fewer. Renderer gate: exit 124,
three `[INFO]`, zero VUID/ERROR/WARN.
